On the excursion algebra
Dennis Gaitsgory, Kevin Lin and Wyatt Reeves

Abstract
. The excursion algebra associated to a scheme X X over 𝔽 q {\mathbb{F}}_{q} and a reductive group 𝖦 {\mathsf{G}} is the algebra of global functions on the stack of arithmetic 𝖦 {\mathsf{G}} -local systems on X X . When X X is a curve, this algebra acts on the space of automorphic functions. In this paper we establish some basic properties of this algebra.



Introduction

In his seminal work
[
VLaf
]
(extended in
[
Xue
]
), V. Lafforgue defined an action
1
1

1

See Sect.
0.1.4
for the precise assertion.

of the
excursion algebra
on the space of automorphic functions. In this paper we study the excursion algebra abstractly, without connection
to automorphic functions (and we do it for an arbitrary algebraic variety
X
over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, not necessarily a curve); we denote it by
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
, where

{\mathsf{G}}
is the target reductive group.

Our main results include:

(1)

Each factor of
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
is reduced and normal;

(2)

In fact,
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
is a product of algebras of the form
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathsf{G}}^{\prime}/\!/\operatorname{Ad}_{\mathsf{g}}({\mathsf{G}}^{\prime})}
, where:

•

{\mathsf{G}}^{\prime}
is a reductive subgroup of
{\mathsf{G}}
;

•

{\mathsf{g}}\in{\mathsf{G}}
is an element that normalizes
{\mathsf{G}}^{\prime}
;

•

\operatorname{Ad}_{\mathsf{g}}
stands for the action by
{\mathsf{g}}
-twisted conjugation.

(3)

\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
is finitely generated over each local Hecke algebra
{\mathcal{H}}_{x}({\mathsf{G}})
(see Theorem
4.4.2
(a) for the precise assertion);

(4)

For
{\mathsf{G}}=GL_{n}
, the map from the global Hecke algebra
{\mathcal{H}}_{X}({\mathsf{G}})
to
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
is surjective (see Theorem
4.4.2
(b) for the precise assertion);

(5)

The algebra
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
is independent of
\ell
in the sense that there exists a canonically defined
{\mathbb{Q}}
-algebra

\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
such that for any
\ell\neq p
, we have a canonical isomorphism

\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}\underset{{\mathbb{Q}}}{\otimes}\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})_{\mathbb{Q}}\simeq\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}}),

and the map
{\mathcal{H}}_{X}({\mathsf{G}})\to\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
is induced by a map
{\mathcal{H}}_{X,{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathsf{G}})\to\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
;

(6)

In particular, for
{\mathsf{G}}=GL_{n}
, the rational structure on
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
is uniquely characterized by the property of being compatible
with the one on
{\mathcal{H}}_{X}({\mathsf{G}})
.

NB: for the validity of (5) and (6) we need to assume that
X
is smooth.

0.1.
The excursion algebra

0.1.1.

Let
X_{0}
be a (geometrically) connected scheme of finite type over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, and let
X
denote its base change to
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
. Let

{\mathsf{G}}
be a reductive group over the field of coefficients
\mathsf{e}:=\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
,
\ell\neq p:=\operatorname{char}({\mathbb{F}}_{q})
.

To this data, following
[
AGKRRV
]
, we associate two objects in derived algebraic geometry over
\mathsf{e}
. One is

\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X)
, see Sect.
3.3.1
for the definition. The prestack
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X)
depends
functorially on the étale site of
X
; hence the geometric Frobenius acting on
X
gives rise to an automorphism,
denoted,
\operatorname{Frob}
, of
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X)
.

We set

\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X):=(\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}.

One shows that
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)
is a union of connected components, each of which is a quasi-compact
algebraic stack over
\mathsf{e}
locally of finite type (in fact, the stack-quotient of an affine scheme by an action of
{\mathsf{G}}
).
Moreover, if one restricts ramification along the divisor at infinity with respect to some compactification (which we will from
now on assume),
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)
has finitely many connected components.

The excursion algebra
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
is defined as the algebra of global functions on
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)
, i.e.,

\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}}):=\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)}).

A priori,
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
is a connective commutative DG algebra over
\mathsf{e}
. However, it follows from the
results of this paper that it is actually
classical
, i.e., is concentrated in cohomological degree
0
.

The goal of this paper is to establish the properties of
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
listed in the preamble.

0.1.2.

Here is some history of the definition of
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
. For the rest of this subsection we will assume that
X_{0}
is a curve; let
\overline{X}_{0}

denote its compactification
2
2

2

We should say that the fact that we consider arbitrary schemes rather than curves
is out of an academic interest–our main applications are in the case of curves.
.

The stack
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)
should be thought of as the moduli space of
continuous
representations
of the Weil group
\operatorname{Weil}(X)
of
X
into
{\mathsf{G}}(\mathsf{e})
up to conjugation.

Let us temporarily replace
\operatorname{Weil}(X)
by an abstract group
\Gamma
, and consider the stack

\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\Gamma,{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}),

and set

\operatorname{Exc}(\Gamma,{\mathsf{G}}):=\Gamma(\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\Gamma,{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}),{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\Gamma,{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})}).

The algebra
\operatorname{Exc}(\Gamma,{\mathsf{G}})
was described explicitly by V. Lafforgue in
[
VLaf
, Sect. 11]
. He exhibited it as a span
of a set of specific elements that he called “excursion operators”, hence the name of the algebra (we recall the definition of these
elements in Sect.
4.3.7
).

The natural projection

\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\Gamma,{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})\to\operatorname{Spec}(\operatorname{Exc}(\Gamma,{\mathsf{G}}))

gives rise to a bijection between:

•

Conjugacy classes of homomorphisms
\Gamma\to{\mathsf{G}}
,
up to semi-simplification
, and

•

\mathsf{e}
-points of
\operatorname{Spec}(\operatorname{Exc}(\Gamma,{\mathsf{G}}))
.

In particular, the set of
\mathsf{e}
-points of
\operatorname{Spec}(\operatorname{Exc}(\Gamma,{\mathsf{G}}))
is in bijection with the set of conjugacy classes
of
semi-simple
homomorphisms
\Gamma\to{\mathsf{G}}
. We refer the reader to
[
VLaf
, Sect 11]
for the discussion
of the relation between
\operatorname{Exc}(\Gamma,{\mathsf{G}})
and
quasi-characters
.

0.1.3.

Let us take
\Gamma
to be
\operatorname{Weil}(X)
, viewed as an abstract group (i.e., we disregard its topology). Then the main construction
in
[
VLaf
]
(extended by C. Xue in
[
Xue
]
) defines an action of

\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{\operatorname{discr}}:=\operatorname{Exc}(\operatorname{Weil}(X),{\mathsf{G}})

on the space of automorphic functions with compact supports for the group
G:={\mathsf{G}}^{\vee}
and the global field corresponding to
\overline{X}_{0}

(with bounded ramification at points of
\overline{X}_{0}-X_{0}
).

We have a naturally defined closed embedding

{}^{\operatorname{cl}}\!\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)\to\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Weil}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}),

which gives rise to a map

\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{\operatorname{discr}}\to\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}}),

which is surjective on
H^{0}(-)
.

In fact, one can describe the corresponding quotient explicitly: the corresponding closed subscheme

\operatorname{Spec}(\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}}))\subset\operatorname{Spec}(\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{\operatorname{discr}})

is the Zariski closure of the subset of
\mathsf{e}
-points that correspond to
continuous

semi-simple homomorphisms
\operatorname{Weil}(X)\to{\mathsf{G}}
, see Proposition
4.2.9
in this paper.

0.1.4.

Combined with the technology developed in
[
AGKRRV
]
, one can show that the action of
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{\operatorname{discr}}

on automorphic functions actually factors via
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
.

0.1.5.

The primary impetus for this paper was to establish properties (1) and (2) mentioned in the preamble:

Property (1) is a key input for the project of proving a version of the Ramanujan-Petersson conjecture over function fields
(unramified version), see
[
Ras
]
.

Property (2) serves as an input for the even more ambitious project of proving the Arthur conjectures, see also
[
Ras
]
.

0.1.6.

Properties (3) and (4) reflect some well-known phenomena on the automorphic side.

Properties (5) and (6) can be regarded as a tiny step towards the program of understanding the phenomenon of
“independence of
\ell
” for the Langlands correspondence over function fields.

0.2.
The geometrically semi-simple locus

The proofs of all the main results of this paper are based on an alternative description of
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
that
we outline in this subsection.

0.2.1.

The prestack
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X)
is a union of connected components
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X)_{\alpha}
;
two
{\mathsf{G}}
-local systems belong to the same connected component if and only if they have isomorphic
semi-simplifications; each
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X)_{\alpha}
contains a unique semi-simple local system
\sigma_{\alpha}

(defined up to an isomorphism).

Denote

\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr},0}(X):=\underset{\alpha}{\sqcup}\operatorname{pt}/{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha},\quad{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}:=\operatorname{Aut}(\sigma_{\alpha});

this is the
semi-simple
locus of
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X)
.

0.2.2.

Set

\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}(X):=(\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr},0}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}.

The main point of this paper is that the restriction map

(0.1)

\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}}):=\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)})\to\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}(X)})=:\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{0}

is an isomorphism. This is our Theorem
3.7.2
.

0.2.3.

The results mentioned in the preamble all follow from the isomorphism (
0.1
).

For example, points (1) and (2) follows from the fact for every
\alpha
for which
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X)_{\alpha}
is
\operatorname{Frob}
-invariant, we have

(\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X)_{\alpha})^{\operatorname{Frob}}\simeq{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}/\operatorname{Ad}_{\operatorname{Frob}}({\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}),

as a stack.

Point (3) follows from the fact that the map

{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}/\!/\operatorname{Ad}_{\operatorname{Frob}}({\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha})\to{\mathsf{G}}/\!/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}),

induced by the inclusion
{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}\to{\mathsf{G}}
, is finite, which is a variant of a theorem from
[
Vin
]
.

Point (5) is obtained by rewriting
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{0}
via Drinfeld’s rational model for the semi-simple envelope of the
Galois group of
X_{0}
, see Sect.
5.2
.

0.2.4.

Let us now explain the mechanism behind the isomorphism (
0.1
).

Recall that the input into the definition of

\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X)
is the symmetric monoidal category
\operatorname{QLisse}(X)
of local systems on
X
. Let
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\subset\operatorname{QLisse}(X)

be the full subcategory generated by local systems that admit a Weil structure.

Denote by
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}(X)
the corresponding substack of
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X)

(in fact,
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}(X)
is the union of certain of the connected components of
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X)
).

It is easy to see that the inclusion

(\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}\to(\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}=:\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)

is an isomorphism.

0.2.5.

Let
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
be the pro-algebraic group, whose representations are finite-dimensional vector spaces
equipped with an automorphism, whose eigenvalues are
q
-Weil numbers.

By definition, we have a homomorphism

{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}

with Zariski-dense image. Denote by
\operatorname{Frob}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
the image of
1\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

In addition, we have a natural homomorphism
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
, which records the decomposition into weights.

0.2.6.

The isomorphism (
0.1
) is based on the following two observations:

•

The prestack
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}(X)
carries a natural action of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
, with
\operatorname{Frob}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}

acting as the automorphism induced by the geometric Frobenius on
X
;

•

The resulting action of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
on
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}(X)
extends to an action of the monoid
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
(that still commutes with

{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
), which contracts it to

\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr,relev,0}}(X):=\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}(X)\cap\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr,0}}(X).

0.2.7.

Let us show how these observations imply the isomorphism (
0.1
).

First, the action of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
on

\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}(X)
induces one on

(\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}\simeq\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X).

Second, the
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
-action on
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}(X)
induces one on
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)
, which contracts it to

\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}(X)
.

In particular, we obtain that
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
carries the following structures:

•

It is a representation of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
;

•

The induced representation of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
extends to a representation of the monoid
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
, and the latter contracts it to
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{0}
;

Note, however, that by the definition of
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)
, the action of
\operatorname{Frob}
on it, and hence on
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
, is trivial.
Since
\operatorname{Frob}
generates
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
, we obtain that the action of all of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
on
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
is actually trivial.

In particular, the action of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
on

\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
is trivial. Hence, the contraction

\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})\to\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{0}

is trivial, i.e., is an isomorphism.

We refer the reader to the proof of Theorem
3.7.2
, where the above argument is spelled out in more detail.

0.2.8.

The idea of a contraction as in Sect.
0.2.6
traces back to the PhD thesis of the third author
of this paper
[
Re
]
, and a subsequent paper by the second and the third authors
[
LR
]
.

In this next subsection we will explain where it comes from.

0.3.
The contraction

0.3.1.

The above structures on the prestack
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}(X)
are induced by the functoriality of the construction
from
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 1.8]
by the corresponding structures on the (symmetric monoidal) category
\operatorname{QLisse}(X)
.

The latter are induced by the corresponding structures on the ambient category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
(defined in Sect.
2.5
),
which we will describe below.

0.3.2.

Let
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)
be the category of mixed Weil sheaves with integral weights, see Sect.
2.4.9
.

We have a natural action of

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})
on
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)
(tensor up by sheaves pulled back from the point-scheme).
We show that
the naturally defined functor

\operatorname{Vect}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)

is an equivalence, see Corollary
2.5.11
.

By the principle of “equivariantization/de-equivariantization” (see (
1.1
)), the latter equivalence implies
that we have a natural action of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
on
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
.

0.3.3.

The extension of the action of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
on
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
to an action of the monoid
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
follows from the next principle:

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be a DG category with an action of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
. Denote
{\mathbf{D}}:={\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
; this is a category acted on by
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})
,
or which is the same by the group
{\mathbb{Z}}
.

In Theorem
1.1.7
we show that the datum of extension of the given
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-action to a
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
-action is equivalent to the datum of
a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-invariant
filtration
on
{\mathbf{D}}
(see Sect.
1.1.4
for what we mean by this).

0.3.4.

In the case of
{\mathbf{C}}:=\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
, the corresponding category
(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X))^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
is the category
introduced in
[
HL
]
, and which we denote by
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X)
. It is defined as

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X),

or which is the same

\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,0}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X),

where

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,0}})\subset{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})

is the full subcategory corresponding to eigenvalues that are Weil numbers of weight
0
.

Using the latter presentation, we define a filtration on
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X)
to be induced by the weight filtration on

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)
.

0.3.5.

The fact that
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
contracts
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}(X)
to
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr,relev,0}}(X)
stems from the following
phenomenon:

In the situation of Sect.
0.3.3
the action of
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
contracts
{\mathbf{C}}
to the category
\operatorname{gr}_{0}({\mathbf{D}})
. In our case,

\operatorname{gr}_{0}(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X))\simeq\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,0}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,0}}(X)=:\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,0}}(X),

where
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,0}}(X)
is the category of Weil sheaves of weight
0
.

In Proposition
2.7.5
we show that
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,0}}(X)
is the
semi-simplification
of the category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
,
and hence the corresponding category
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev,0}}(X)
is the semi-simplification of
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
.
The prestack
associated to the (symmetric monoidal) category
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev,0}}(X)
by the construction of
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 1.8]
is
exactly
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr,relev,0}}(X)
.

0.4.
Structure of the paper

0.4.1.

The goal of Sect.
1
is to prove Theorem
1.1.7
, which describes what kind of structure on a category

{\mathbf{C}}
makes it into a comodule over
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})
. As was mentioned already, this structure turns out to
be a filtration on the corresponding category
{\mathbf{D}}:={\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
.

Note that this is vaguely analogous to the fact that the datum of a filtration on a vector space
V

is equivalent to realizing
V
as the fiber at
1
of a quasi-coherent sheaf on
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.

0.4.2.

The goal of Sect.
2
is to carry out the main construction of this paper, which is formulated as
Theorem
2.8.2
: it says that the category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
admits a canonical
action of
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
that contracts it onto
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)
.

Prior to doing so, we introduce the main actors:

(1)

The groups

{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0};

(2)

The various versions of the category of Weil sheaves:

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)

(so that for
X=\operatorname{pt}
we recover the categories of representations of the groups in the previous
item);

(3)

The categories

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X),\,\,\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X)\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X),

(4)

The relation between the categories in items (2) and (3) is via the procedure of equivariantization and de-equivariantization
with respect to the groups in item (1).

0.4.3.

In Sect.
3
we introduce the counterparts of the categories in items (2) and (3) above, where
we impose the condition that the underlying object of
\operatorname{Shv}(X)
be
lisse
:

\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X)\subset\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\subset\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)\subset\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X),

and

\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X),\,\,\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X)\text{ and }\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X).

We introduce the corresponding Galois groups that control their hearts:

•

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)\leftrightarrow\operatorname{QLisse}(X)
;

•

\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)\leftrightarrow\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)
;

•

\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg,loc.fin}}(X)\leftrightarrow\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)
;

•

\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}(X)\leftrightarrow\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)
;

•

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev}}(X)\leftrightarrow\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
;

•

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev},0}(X)\leftrightarrow\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)
; we show that
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev},0}(X)
is the maximal reductive quotient of

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev}}(X)
;

•

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,gr}}(X)\leftrightarrow\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X)
.

We also introduce the corresponding versions of the moduli space of local systems:

•

\operatorname{QLisse}(X)\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X)
;

•

\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}(X)
;

•

\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr,relev},0}(X)
;

•

\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X):=(\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}\simeq(\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}
;

•

{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0})}{\otimes}\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X)\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}(X):=(\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr,relev},0}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}
; note that the Galois group responsible for this
category is what we denote
\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{red}}(X)
, see Sect.
5.2.1
.

We apply Theorem-Construction
2.8.2
to the full subcategory

\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
and obtain a contraction of the prestack

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}(X)
onto
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev},0}(X)
.

Finally, we introduce the excursion algebra
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
as

\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)}),

and prove the main result of this paper, Theorem
3.7.2
, which says that the map

\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)})\to\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}(X)})

is an isomorphism.

0.4.4.

In Sect.
4
we connect our definition of the excursion algebra
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
to (a variant of)
V. Lafforgue’s definition, which uses the Weil group of
X
as a discrete group.

We then establish properties (3) and (4) stated in the preamble.

As was mentioned already, property (3) is deduced from Theorem
3.7.2
using a variant of a theorem of Vinberg.

Property (4) is deduced from Property (3) using Chebotarev’s density. It is a variation (explained to us by V. Lafforgue)
of the argument from
[
Laum
]
, which shows that the map from the Grothendieck group of Weil sheaves on
X
to
the space of
\mathsf{e}
-valued functions on all
X({\mathbb{F}}_{q^{n}})
is injective.

0.4.5.

In Sect.
5
we establish Properties (5) and (6) stated in the preamble.

We again use Theorem
3.7.2
, which allows us to interpret
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
as the algebra of global functions on the stack

\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{red}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}),

where
\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{red}}(X)
as in Sect.
5.2.1
.

The next observation is that the above pro-reductive group
\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{red}}(X)
is closely related to the
pro-semi simple completion
\operatorname{Gal-Drinf}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)
of
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)
studied by V. Drinfeld in
[
Dr
]
.

The main result of
loc. cit.
says that
\operatorname{Gal-Drinf}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)
(which is a priori a pro-semi simple group over
\mathsf{e}
)
has a rational structure as an object of Drinfeld’s category
\operatorname{Pro-ss}(\mathsf{e})
. This category is a rather coarse object; however,
the rational structure on
\operatorname{Gal-Drinf}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)
turns out to be exactly enough to induce a rational structure on
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
.

A small complication arises in establishing the
continuity
of the
\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{\mathbb{Q}})
-action on the model of

\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
over
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
(an issue that is not explicitly addressed in
[
Dr
]
). We deal with it in Sects.

5.7
-
5.8
.

0.5.
Conventions and notation

The conventions in this paper follow those of
[
AGKRRV
]
. Let us recap the following two points:

0.5.1.

We will be working with the
(\infty,2)
-category
\operatorname{DGCat}
of
\mathsf{e}
-linear DG categories;
see
[
GRV
, Sect. A.2]
for a detailed discussion. This
(\infty,2)
-category carries a symmetric
monoidal structure (the Lurie tensor product); the unit for it is the DG category
\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}
of chain
complexes of
\mathsf{e}
-vector spaces.

For an algebra (resp., coalgebra) object
{\mathbf{A}}\in\operatorname{DGCat}
, we let
{\mathbf{A}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
(resp.,
{\mathbf{A}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}
) denote
the category of
{\mathbf{A}}
-modules (resp., comodules) in
\operatorname{DGCat}
.

0.5.2.

There will be two kinds of algebraic geometry in this paper:

One is taking place over the ground field
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
,
and the main actor will be a fixed scheme
X_{0}
.
We will be interested in various categories of (ind-constructible) sheaves on
X:=\operatorname{Spec}(\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q})\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathbb{F}}_{q})}{\times}X_{0}
;
these will be objects of
\operatorname{DGCat}
. Derived algebraic geometry will play no role here.

The other algebraic geometry is taking place over
\mathsf{e}
and the objects that we will be working with are
inherently derived; our principal actors, such as
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{restr}}(X)
and
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)
are examples.

0.6.
Acknowledgements

We wish to thank A. Beilinson, P. Boisseau, R. van Dobben de Bruyn, A. Eteve, V. Lafforgue, S. Raskin, J. Scholbach, P. Scholze and C. Xue
for very helpful discussions.

The main part of this work was done while the second author was visiting MPIM in summer 2025.

1.
Categories with an action of the monoid
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}

The goal of this section is to state and then prove Theorem
1.1.7
.

1.1.
Statement of the result

1.1.1.

Recall that the operations of equivariantization and de-equivariantization

{\mathbf{C}}\mapsto{\mathbf{C}}^{H}\text{ and }{\mathbf{D}}\mapsto\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(H)}{\otimes}{\mathbf{D}}

give rise to mutually inverse equivalences

(1.1)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(H)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(H)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}

for any algebraic group
H
, where
(-)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
and
(-)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}
are taken in
\operatorname{DGCat}
.

The equivalence (
1.1
) is symmetric monoidal, where we endow the left-hand side with
the symmetric monoidal structure compatible with the forgetful functor

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(H)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}\to\operatorname{DGCat},

and the right-hand side with tensor product over
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(H)
.

1.1.2.

We take
H={\mathbb{G}}_{m}
, and obtain an equivalence

(1.2)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},

where we have identified

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},

since
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})
is

{\mathbb{Z}}\times\operatorname{Vect}\simeq\operatorname{Vect}^{{\mathbb{Z}}}

as a (symmetric monoidal) DG category.

1.1.3.

The question that we want to address in this section is the following: what is the counterpart of
(
1.2
) if we replace the
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
in the left-hand side by the monoid

{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
?

1.1.4.

Let
\operatorname{DGCat}^{\operatorname{Filtr}}
denote the category whose objects are DG categories
{\mathbf{D}}
equipped with a sequence
of full subcategories

{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n}\overset{i_{\leq n}}{\hookrightarrow}{\mathbf{D}},\quad n\in{\mathbb{Z}}

such that:

•

{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n}\subset{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n+1}
;

•

Each
i_{n}
admits a continuous right adjoint;

•

The functor
\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n}\to{\mathbf{D}}
is an equivalence.

Morphisms in this category are functors
G:{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime\prime}
that map the corresponding subcategories
to one another, and such that the Beck-Chevalley conditions hold, i.e., for every
n
, the natural transformation

(1.3)

G_{\leq n}\circ i_{\leq n}^{R}\to i_{\leq n}^{R}\circ G

is an isomorphism, where
G_{\leq n}
denotes the induced functor
{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}_{\leq n}\to{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime\prime}_{\leq n}
.

1.1.5.

Let
{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{Filtr}}
be the full subcategory in

{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\underset{\operatorname{DGCat}}{\times}\operatorname{DGCat}^{\operatorname{Filtr}},

whose objects consist of

({\mathbf{D}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},{\mathbf{D}}_{i}\hookrightarrow{\mathbf{D}}),

for which the action of the generator
1\in{\mathbb{Z}}
maps
{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n}
isomorphically onto
{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n+1}
.

1.1.6.

We will prove the following theorem:

Theorem 1.1.7
.

There is a canonical equivalence

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{Filtr}}

that makes the diagram

(1.4)

\begin{CD}{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}@>{\sim}>{}>{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{Filtr}}\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}@>{\sim}>{}>{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\end{CD}

commute, where the vertical arrows are the forgetful functors.

Remark 1.1.8
.

Both sides in Theorem
1.1.7
carry naturally defined symmetric monoidal structures, compatible with the vertical
functors in (
1.4
). It will
follow from the construction that the equivalence in Theorem
1.1.7
is symmetric monoidal.

1.1.9.
Example

Let
A
be a non-negatively graded associative algebra. The resulting coaction of
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
on
A
, which is a
homomorphism

A\to A[t],

gives rise to a functor

A{\operatorname{-mod}}\to A[t]{\operatorname{-mod}}\simeq A{\operatorname{-mod}}\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}),

which makes
A{\operatorname{-mod}}
into an object of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}
.
Let us describe the corresponding object of
{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{Filtr}}
.

First, the underlying object of
{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
is
A{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{gr}}
, the
category of graded
A
-modules.

Let
A{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{gr}}_{\leq n}
be the full subcategory generated by
A(m)
for
m\leq n
, where

A(m)_{i}=A_{m+i}
. Informally,
A{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{gr}}_{\leq n}
consists of objects generated by
elements of degrees
\leq n
.

Equivalently,
A{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{gr}}_{\leq n}
is the
left
orthogonal of the full subcategory consisting
of modules that are concentrated in degrees
>n
.

Then the object of
{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{Filtr}}
corresponding to
A{\operatorname{-mod}}
under the equivalence of Theorem
1.1.7
is

\{A{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{gr}}_{\leq n}\}.

1.2.
The action of zero

1.2.1.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be an object of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}
. Taking the fiber of the coaction functor

{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})

at
0
, we obtain an endofunctor of
{\mathbf{C}}
that we denote by
[0]
.

Let us describe this endofunctor explicitly in terms of the equivalence of Theorem
1.1.7
.

1.2.2.

Set
{\mathbf{D}}:={\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
. The endofunctor
[0]
respects the action of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
, and thus
gives rise to an endofunctor of
{\mathbf{D}}
; we denote it by the same character
[0]
.

The questions of describing the above two versions of
[0]
are equivalent under
equivariantization and de-equivariantization, so it is sufficient to deal with the latter.

1.2.3.

By Theorem
1.1.7
,
{\mathbf{D}}
upgrades to an object of
{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{Filtr}}
.

For
n\in{\mathbb{Z}}
denote by
j_{\geq n}
the
right adjoint
of the projection

{\mathbf{D}}\to{\mathbf{D}}/{\mathbf{D}}_{<n}=:{\mathbf{D}}_{\geq n}.

Similarly, denote by
j_{=n}
the
right adjoint
of the projection

{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n}\to{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n}/{\mathbf{D}}_{<n}=:{\mathbf{D}}_{=n}.

1.2.4.

Denote

\operatorname{gr}({\mathbf{D}}):=\underset{n}{\oplus}\,{\mathbf{D}}_{=n}\simeq\underset{n}{\Pi}\,{\mathbf{D}}_{=n}.

Note that the action of
{\mathbb{Z}}
on
{\mathbf{D}}
identifies

\operatorname{gr}({\mathbf{D}})\simeq({\mathbf{D}}_{=0})^{{\mathbb{Z}}}.

Denote by
\phi_{\bullet}
the functor

\operatorname{gr}({\mathbf{D}})\to{\mathbf{D}},

whose
n
th component
\phi_{n}
is
i_{\leq n}\circ j_{=n}
.

Denote by
\psi_{\bullet}
the functor

{\mathbf{D}}\to\operatorname{gr}({\mathbf{D}}),

whose
n
th component
\psi_{n}
is
j_{=n}^{L}\circ i^{R}_{\leq n}
.

Note that the functors
\phi_{\bullet}
and
\psi_{\bullet}
are compatible with the action of
{\mathbb{Z}}
on the two sides.

Remark 1.2.5
.

Note that although each
\phi_{n}
is fully faithful, the functor
\phi_{\bullet}
is not:
the essential images of the different
\phi_{n}
in
{\mathbf{D}}
interact non-trivially.

1.2.6.

The following will be proved along with Theorem
1.1.7
:

Proposition 1.2.7
.

The action of
[0]
on
{\mathbf{D}}
is given by the composition

{\mathbf{D}}\overset{\psi_{\bullet}}{\to}\operatorname{gr}({\mathbf{D}})\overset{\phi_{\bullet}}{\to}{\mathbf{D}}.

1.2.8.

Recall that
{\mathbf{D}}
was obtained as
{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
. Let

\phi:{\mathbf{D}}_{0}\to{\mathbf{C}}\text{ and }\psi:{\mathbf{C}}\to{\mathbf{D}}_{0}

be the functors corresponding to
\phi_{\bullet}
and
\psi_{\bullet}
by
the equivalence (
1.2
).

Applying to Proposition
1.2.7
the equivalence (
1.2
), we obtain:

Corollary 1.2.9
.

The action of
[0]
on
{\mathbf{C}}
factors as

{\mathbf{C}}\overset{\psi}{\to}{\mathbf{D}}_{0}\overset{\phi}{\to}{\mathbf{C}}.

In what follows we will refer to
{\mathbf{D}}_{0}
as the
attracting category
corresponding to
{\mathbf{C}}
. Note that by construction,
the endofunctor

\psi\circ\phi

of
{\mathbf{D}}_{0}
is canonically isomorphic to the identity.

1.2.10.

Note that the assignment

({\mathbf{C}}\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod})\mapsto({\mathbf{D}}_{0}\in\operatorname{DGCat})

has a structure of right lax symmetric monoidal functor. In particular, it
maps (commutative) algebra objects in
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}
to
(commutative) algebra objects in
\operatorname{DGCat}
, i.e., (symmetric) monoidal
DG categories.

It will follow from the construction that the functors
\phi
and
\psi
appearing in Corollary
1.2.9
,
viewed as natural transformations between functors

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}\leftrightarrow\operatorname{DGCat},

have natural symmetric monoidal structures, and the identification in Corollary
1.2.9
is compatible
with these structures.

In particular, if
{\mathbf{C}}
is a (commutative) algebra object in
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}
,
and hence
{\mathbf{D}}_{0}
is a (symmetric) monoidal DG category, the functors
\psi
and
\phi

have natural symmetric monoidal structures, and the identification of Corollary
1.2.9
is compatible
with these structures.

1.2.11.

Let us see what Corollary
1.2.9
says in the example in Sect.
1.1.9
. Let
A_{0}
be the degree
0
-component of
A
.
We have the natural homomorphisms

A\to A_{0}\text{ and }A_{0}\to A.

The endofunctor
[0]
of
A{\operatorname{-mod}}
acts as

A{\operatorname{-mod}}\overset{A_{0}\underset{A}{\otimes}(-)}{\longrightarrow}A_{0}{\operatorname{-mod}}\overset{A\underset{A_{0}}{\otimes}(-)}{\longrightarrow}A{\operatorname{-mod}}.

We identify

A{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{gr}}_{=n}\simeq A_{0}{\operatorname{-mod}}

for any
n
.

It is easy to see that under this identification, the functor

\phi_{n}:A_{0}{\operatorname{-mod}}\to A{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{gr}}

corresponds to

A_{0}{\operatorname{-mod}}\overset{\operatorname{deg}=n}{\longrightarrow}A_{0}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{gr}}\overset{A\underset{A_{0}}{\otimes}(-)}{\longrightarrow}A{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{gr}},

and the functor

\psi_{n}:A{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{gr}}\to A_{0}{\operatorname{-mod}}

to

A{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{gr}}\overset{A_{0}\underset{A}{\otimes}(-)}{\longrightarrow}A_{0}{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{gr}}\overset{\operatorname{deg}=n}{\longrightarrow}A_{0}{\operatorname{-mod}}.

From here, we obtain that the functors
\phi
and
\psi
identify with

A_{0}{\operatorname{-mod}}\overset{A\underset{A_{0}}{\otimes}(-)}{\longrightarrow}A{\operatorname{-mod}}

and

A{\operatorname{-mod}}\overset{A_{0}\underset{A}{\otimes}(-)}{\longrightarrow}A_{0}{\operatorname{-mod}},

respectively.

1.3.
Proof of Theorem
1.1.7
, Step 1

1.3.1.

Note that when
H
is commutative, the equivalence (
1.1
) is compatible with another pair of
symmetric monoidal structures, where:

•

In the left-hand side, we take

{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{2}\mapsto({\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{1}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{2})^{H},

which carries an action of
H
through either of the factors due to the
commutativity of
H
;

•

In the left hand side we take

{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{1}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{2},

with the action of
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(H)
given via the (symmetric) monoidal functor

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(H)\to{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(H)\otimes{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(H)

that arises as pullback along the multiplication map

\operatorname{pt}/H\times\operatorname{pt}/H\to\operatorname{pt}/H,

which exists thanks to the commutativity of
H
.

1.3.2.

Let us regard (
1.2
) as a symmetric monoidal equivalence with respect to the
symmetric monoidal structures specified in Sect.
1.3.1
.

Note that under the equivalence

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},

this is the symmetric monoidal structure compatible with the forgetful functor

(1.5)

{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}\to\operatorname{DGCat}.

I.e., the monoidal operation is given by

{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{1},{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{2}\mapsto{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime}_{1}\otimes{\mathbf{C}}^{\prime\prime}_{2},

equipped with the diagonal action of
{\mathbb{Z}}
.

1.3.3.

The operation of pullback along

{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\overset{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}{\times}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}

makes
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})
into a coalgebra in
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}

with respect to the (symmetric) monoidal structure in Sect.
1.3.1
.

We have a tautological equivalence

(1.6)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}\simeq{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}({\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}).

1.3.4.

Under the equivalence (
1.2
), the coalgebra object

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})\in{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}

corresponds to

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\in{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod},

with the coalgebra structure given by pullback along

({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\times({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}.

Applying (
1.2
), we obtain an equivalence

(1.7)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}({\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod})\simeq{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}({\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}).

1.3.5.

Applying the forgetful functor (
1.5
), we can view
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
as a coalgebra
in
\operatorname{DGCat}
.

We will prove an equivalence:

(1.8)

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}\simeq\operatorname{DGCat}^{\operatorname{Filtr}},

compatible with the forgetful functors of both sides to
\operatorname{DGCat}
.

The compatibility with the
{\mathbb{Z}}
-actions will follow from the construction, and that would
lead to an equivalence

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}({\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod})\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{Filtr}}.

Composing this with (
1.6
) and (
1.7
), we obtain the statement of Theorem
1.1.7
.

1.4.
Proof of Theorem
1.1.7
, Step 2

1.4.1.

Let
\operatorname{Filtr}
denote the endofunctor on
\operatorname{DGCat}
that sends

{\mathbf{C}}\mapsto\operatorname{Filtr}({\mathbf{C}}):=\operatorname{Funct}({\mathbb{Z}},{\mathbf{C}}),

where we view
{\mathbb{Z}}
as a poset and hence a category.

Note that
\operatorname{Filtr}
has a natural comonad structure:

The binary operation is defined by

\displaystyle\operatorname{Funct}({\mathbb{Z}},{\mathbf{C}})\to\operatorname{Funct}({\mathbb{Z}},\operatorname{Funct}({\mathbb{Z}},{\mathbf{C}}))=\operatorname{Funct}({\mathbb{Z}}\times{\mathbb{Z}},{\mathbf{C}}),

\displaystyle\{{\mathbf{c}}_{n}\}\in\operatorname{Funct}({\mathbb{Z}},{\mathbf{C}})\mapsto\{{\mathbf{c}}_{n_{1},n_{2}}\}\in\operatorname{Funct}({\mathbb{Z}}\times{\mathbb{Z}},{\mathbf{C}}),\quad{\mathbf{c}}_{n_{1},n_{2}}:={\mathbf{c}}_{\operatorname{min}(n_{1},n_{2})}.

The counit is given by

\operatorname{Funct}({\mathbb{Z}},{\mathbf{C}})\to{\mathbf{C}},\quad\{{\mathbf{c}}_{n}\}\in\operatorname{Funct}({\mathbb{Z}},{\mathbf{C}})\mapsto\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathbf{c}}_{n}.

1.4.2.

Recall that for
{\mathbf{C}}\in\operatorname{DGCat}
, the category

{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})

identifies canonically with
\operatorname{Filtr}({\mathbf{C}})
, see, e.g.,
[
GaRo2
, Chapter 9, Sect. 1.3]
.

We claim:

Proposition 1.4.3
.

The isomorphism of endofunctors of
\operatorname{DGCat}

(1.9)

\operatorname{Filtr}(-)\simeq(-)\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})

upgrades to an isomorphism of comonads, where the comonad structure
on the right-hand side comes from the coalgebra structure on
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
.

Proof.

Note that the comonad
\operatorname{Filtr}
on
\operatorname{DGCat}
commutes with tensor products
by objects of
\operatorname{DGCat}
. Hence, it is also given by tensoring with a coalgebra
object in
\operatorname{DGCat}
, to be denoted
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})^{\prime}
.

It follows from (
1.9
) that the plain object of
\operatorname{DGCat}
underlying
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})^{\prime}

identifies with
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})
. Thus, it remains to show that the two comonad structures
agree.

It follows by direct inspection that the two binary operations

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\rightrightarrows{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})

agree.

We claim that this implies that the entire comonad structures agree. Indeed, note that both structures are
given by t-exact functors, and the categories involved are derived categories of their hearts.

∎

1.4.4.

Thus, we obtain that in order to prove (
1.8
), we need to establish an equivalence

(1.10)

\operatorname{Filtr}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})\simeq\operatorname{DGCat}^{\operatorname{Filtr}},

compatible with the forgetful functors to
\operatorname{DGCat}
.

1.5.
Proof of Theorem
1.1.7
, Step 3

1.5.1.

We claim that the forgetful functor

{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Filtr}}:\operatorname{DGCat}^{\operatorname{Filtr}}\to\operatorname{DGCat}

admits a right adjoint, to be denoted
{\mathbf{ind}}_{\operatorname{Filtr}}
.

Namely,
{\mathbf{ind}}_{\operatorname{Filtr}}
sends
{\mathbf{C}}\in\operatorname{DGCat}
to an object of
\operatorname{DGCat}^{\operatorname{Filtr}}
, whose underlying DG category
is
\operatorname{Funct}({\mathbb{Z}},{\mathbf{C}})
, and where

\operatorname{Funct}({\mathbb{Z}},{\mathbf{C}})_{\leq n}:=(\{{\mathbf{c}}_{n}\}\,\mid\,{\mathbf{c}}_{m}\to{\mathbf{c}}_{m+1}\text{ are isomorphisms for }m\geq n).

1.5.2.

Let us construct the adjunction data. The counit of the adjunction, which is a functor

\operatorname{Funct}({\mathbb{Z}},{\mathbf{C}})\to{\mathbf{C}},

is given by

\{{\mathbf{c}}_{n}\}\mapsto\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathbf{c}}_{n}.

The unit of the adjunction, which is a functor,

{\mathbf{D}}\mapsto\operatorname{Funct}({\mathbb{Z}},{\mathbf{D}}),\quad{\mathbf{D}}\in\operatorname{DGCat}^{\operatorname{Filtr}},

is given by

{\mathbf{d}}\mapsto\{{\mathbf{d}}_{n}\},\quad{\mathbf{d}}_{n}:=i_{\leq n}\circ i_{\leq n}^{R}({\mathbf{d}}).

1.5.3.

It follows from the construction that the comonad on
\operatorname{DGCat}
attached to the above adjunction
identifies with
\operatorname{Filtr}
.

This gives rise to a functor

\operatorname{DGCat}^{\operatorname{Filtr}}\to\operatorname{Filtr}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat}).

In order to show that the functor is an equivalence, we have to show that the functor
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Filtr}}

satisfies the assumptions of the (comonadic) Barr-Beck-Lurie theorem.

1.5.4.

First, we claim that
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Filtr}}
is conservative.

Let
G:{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}\to{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime\prime}
be an equivalence, and we need to show that it induces an equivalence

{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime}_{\leq n}\to{\mathbf{D}}^{\prime\prime}_{\leq n}.

However, this follows from the Beck-Chevalley isomorphisms (
1.3
).

1.5.5.

We now claim that
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Filtr}}
commutes with all limits. Let

\alpha\mapsto({\mathbf{D}}_{\alpha},\{{\mathbf{D}}_{\alpha,\leq n}\})

be a diagram in
\operatorname{DGCat}^{\operatorname{Filtr}}
.

Set

{\mathbf{D}}:=\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathbf{D}}_{\alpha}\text{ and }{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n}:=\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathbf{D}}_{\alpha,\leq n}.

The adjoint functors

i_{\alpha,\leq n}:{\mathbf{D}}_{\alpha,\leq n}\rightleftarrows{\mathbf{D}}_{\alpha}:(i_{\alpha,\leq n})^{R}

give rise to adjoint functors

i_{\leq n}:{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n}\rightleftarrows{\mathbf{D}}:(i_{\leq n})^{R},

and both commute with

{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n}\to{\mathbf{D}}_{\alpha,\leq n}\text{ and }{\mathbf{D}}\to{\mathbf{D}}_{\alpha}.

We claim that
({\mathbf{D}},\{{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n}\})
is indeed an object of
\operatorname{DGCat}^{\operatorname{Filtr}}
. For that we need to show that
the functor

\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n}\to{\mathbf{D}}

is an equivalence.

By
[
GaRo1
, Chapter 1, Proposition 2.5.7]
, this is equivalent to

{\mathbf{D}}\to\underset{n}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n}

being an equivalence, where the limit is taken with respect to the right adjoints of
{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n}\to{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n+1}
.

The required assertion follows now from the commutative diagram

\begin{CD}{\mathbf{D}}@>{}>{}>\underset{n}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n}\\
@V{=}V{}V@V{}V{=}V\\
\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathbf{D}}_{\alpha}@>{}>{}>\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\underset{n}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathbf{D}}_{\alpha,\leq n}\simeq\underset{n}{\operatorname{lim}}\,\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathbf{D}}_{\alpha,\leq n},\end{CD}

where the bottom horizontal arrow is an equivalence again by
[
GaRo1
, Chapter 1, Proposition 2.5.7]
.

It is clear that
({\mathbf{D}},\{{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n}\})
constructed above is the limit of
({\mathbf{D}}_{\alpha},\{{\mathbf{D}}_{\alpha,\leq n}\})
in

\operatorname{DGCat}^{\operatorname{Filtr}}
. By construction

{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Filtr}}({\mathbf{D}},\{{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n}\})={\mathbf{D}}=\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{lim}}\,{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Filtr}}({\mathbf{D}}_{\alpha},\{{\mathbf{D}}_{\alpha,\leq n}\}),

as required.

∎[Theorem
1.1.7
]

1.5.6.

Unwinding, we obtain that the functor

\operatorname{Filtr}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})\to\operatorname{DGCat}^{\operatorname{Filtr}}

is given as follows.

It sends

({\mathbf{C}},{\mathbf{C}}\overset{\Phi}{\to}\operatorname{Funct}({\mathbb{Z}},{\mathbf{C}}))\in\operatorname{Filtr}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}(\operatorname{DGCat})

to the object

({\mathbf{D}},\{{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n}\})\in\operatorname{DGCat}^{\operatorname{Filtr}},

where
{\mathbf{D}}={\mathbf{C}}
, and
{\mathbf{D}}_{\leq n}=:{\mathbf{C}}_{\leq n}
is the full subcategory of
{\mathbf{C}}
consisting of
those objects
{\mathbf{c}}
, for which the map

\Phi({\mathbf{c}})_{n^{\prime}}\to\underset{m}{\operatorname{colim}}\,\Phi({\mathbf{c}})_{m}={\mathbf{c}}

is an isomorphism for all
n^{\prime}\geq n
.

1.6.
Identifying the action of zero

We will now show that the equivalence established in Theorem
1.1.7
has the property stated in Proposition
1.2.7
.

1.6.1.

Note that for
{\mathbf{C}}\in\operatorname{DGCat}
, the functor

\operatorname{Filtr}({\mathbf{C}}):=\operatorname{Funct}({\mathbb{Z}},{\mathbf{C}})\simeq{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\iota^{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{C}}\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{pt}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\simeq{\mathbf{C}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}}

(where
\iota
is the embedding
\operatorname{pt}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
) is the functor of the
associated graded

\operatorname{gr}(\{{\mathbf{c}}_{n}\})=\{\operatorname{cofib}({\mathbf{c}}_{n-1}\to{\mathbf{c}}_{n})\}.

1.6.2.

For
{\mathbf{D}}\in\operatorname{DGCat}^{\operatorname{Filtr}}
, the corresponding object in
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}
has
{\mathbf{D}}
as the underlying category
and the coaction

{\mathbf{D}}\to{\mathbf{D}}\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\simeq\operatorname{Funct}({\mathbb{Z}},{\mathbf{D}})

is given by

{\mathbf{d}}\mapsto\{i_{\leq n}\circ i_{\leq n}^{R}({\mathbf{d}})\}.

Combining with Sect.
1.6.1
, we obtain that the functor
[0]
, which in the present context is the functor

(1.11)

{\mathbf{D}}\to{\mathbf{D}}\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\overset{\operatorname{Id}\otimes\iota^{*}}{\longrightarrow}{\mathbf{D}}\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\operatorname{pt}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\simeq{\mathbf{D}}^{{\mathbb{Z}}}

is given by

{\mathbf{d}}\mapsto\{\operatorname{cofib}(i_{\leq n-1}\circ i_{\leq n-1}^{R}({\mathbf{d}})\to i_{\leq n}\circ i_{\leq n}^{R}({\mathbf{d}}))\}.

1.6.3.

Finally, we note that for every
n
, we have

\operatorname{cofib}(i_{\leq n-1}\circ i_{\leq n-1}^{R}(-)\to i_{\leq n}\circ i_{\leq n}^{R}(-))\simeq\phi_{n}\circ\psi_{n},

where
\phi_{n}
and
\psi_{n}
are as in Sect.
1.2.4
.

2.
A contraction on the category of Weil sheaves

The goal of this section is to state and prove Theorem-Construction
2.8.2
. In order to do so, we will
need to introduce a little zoo of various versions of the category of Weil sheaves.

2.1.
Various algebro-geometric completions of
{\mathbb{Z}}

In this subsection we will introduce the pro-algebraic groups

{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}},\,\,{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}\text{ and }{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0},

which would play an important role throughout the paper: the will serve as “equivariantization/de-equivariantization” agents.

2.1.1.

Let
{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}
be the category of representations of
{\mathbb{Z}}
. We consider
it as a symmetric monoidal category under tensor product, so that the forgetful functor

{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}

is symmetric monoidal.

We have

(2.1)

{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}\simeq{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m}),

where we consider
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})
as a symmetric monoidal category with respect to
convolution
.
Under this equivalence, the forgetful functor
{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}\to\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}
corresponds to the functor

\Gamma({\mathbb{G}}_{m},-):{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})\to\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}.

Let

{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{loc.fin}}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}

be the full subcategory that consists of locally finite representations.
Under (
2.1
) it corresponds to the full subcategory

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})_{\operatorname{tors}}\subset{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})

consisting of quasi-coherent sheaves, whose set-theoretic support lies in the union of closed points of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.

The category
{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{loc.fin}}
together with the forgetful functor to
\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}
corresponds
to a pro-algebraic group over
\mathsf{e}
, which we denote by
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}}
.

2.1.2.

Recall that an element of
\mathsf{e}:=\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}_{\ell}
is called a
q
-
Weil number
of weight
n
if:

(1)

It belongs to
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}
;

(2)

Its image under any embedding
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\to{\mathbb{C}}
has absolute value
q^{n/2}
;

(3)

It is an algebraic integer outside of
p
(i.e., becomes an algebraic integer after multiplication by
p^{m}
for some
m
).

It is easy to see that any Weil number is actually a
unit
outside of
p
(indeed, take its norm and we obtain a
rational number with norm a power of
p
).

We define the notion of
mock

q
-Weil number by omitting condition (3) in the definition of Weil numbers.

2.1.3.

Let

{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{wt}}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{loc.fin}}

be the full subcategory, consisting of objects, on which the eigenvalues of the generator
1\in{\mathbb{Z}}
are
q
-Weil numbers
in
\mathsf{e}
. This is a symmetric monoidal subcategory because Weil numbers form a subgroup of
\mathsf{e}^{\times}
.

Under (
2.1
), it corresponds to the full subcategory

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})_{\operatorname{wt}}\subset{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})_{\operatorname{tors}}

consisting of quasi-coherent sheaves, whose set-theoretic support belongs to the set of
q
-Weil numbers.

2.1.4.

The category
{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{wt}}
together with the forgetful functor to
\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}
corresponds
to a pro-algebraic group over
\mathsf{e}
, which we denote by
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
.

By construction,
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
comes equipped with the following structures:

•

The inclusion
{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{wt}}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}
gives rise to a
homomorphism
{\mathbb{Z}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
with Zariski-dense image; we denote by
\operatorname{Frob}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}

the image of the generator
1\in{\mathbb{Z}}
.

•

We have a canonical homomorphism
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
; it corresponds to the
decomposition of
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{wt}}
according to weights;
a choice of
q^{\frac{1}{2}}\in\mathsf{e}
gives rise to a left inverse of the above map.

Remark 2.1.5
.

Note the following key difference between
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
and
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}}
: the former
is defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}\subset\mathsf{e}
, whereas the latter is not.

2.1.6.

Let
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0}
denote the quotient
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
. Note that

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0})
is a full subcategory of

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})={\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{wt}}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{loc.fin}}\subset{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}

consisting of modules, in which the generator
1\in{\mathbb{Z}}
acts with generalized eigenvalues that are

q
-Weil numbers of weight
0
.

Equivalently,

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0})\subset{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})={\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})_{\operatorname{wt}}\subset{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})_{\operatorname{tors}}\subset{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})

is the subcategory

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})_{0}\subset{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})

that consists of quasi-coherent sheaves, whose set-theoretic support belongs to the set of
q
-Weil numbers
of weight
0
.

2.1.7.

Here are some more remarks about the structure of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
:

•

Its unipotent factor is isomorphic to
{\mathbb{G}}_{a}
; indeed, its category of representations is
the subcategory
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})_{\{1\}}\subset{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})
of quasi-coherent sheaves
set-theoretically supported at
1\in{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
;

•

\pi_{0}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})\simeq\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}
; indeed its characters are the roots of unity in
\mathsf{e}
;

•

The reductive part of the connected component of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0}
is the pro-torus,
whose group of characters is the quotient of the group of Weil numbers of weight
0
by roots
of unity.

2.1.8.

Let
\mathbb{H}
be
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}}
,
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
or
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0}
.
Write

\mathbb{H}\simeq\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{lim}}\,H_{\alpha},

where
H_{\alpha}
are finite-dimensional algebraic groups.

We claim:

Proposition 2.1.9
.

(a) The category
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\mathbb{H})
is compactly generated by finite-dimensional representations;

(b) The monoidal category
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\mathbb{H})
is rigid;

(c) The functor

(2.2)

\underset{\alpha}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(H_{\alpha})\to{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\mathbb{H})

is an equivalence.

Proof.

Point (a) is obvious from the description of
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\mathbb{H})
as a full subcategory in
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})_{\operatorname{tors}}
.

Point (b) follows from the fact that compact objects in
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\mathbb{H})
are dualizable (which in turn follows from point (a)). Point (c)
holds for any pro-algebraic group for which (a) holds:

Indeed, it suffices to show that the functor (
2.2
) is fully faithful, and given (a) it suffices to check that it
is fully faithful on compact objects. I.e., it suffices
to show that for an index
\alpha_{0}
,
i\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\geq 0}
and
V_{1},V_{2}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(H_{\alpha_{0}})^{\heartsuit}
, the map

\underset{\alpha\geq\alpha_{0}}{\operatorname{colim}}\,{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Ext}}}^{i}_{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(H_{\alpha})}(\operatorname{Res}^{H_{\alpha_{0}}}_{H_{\alpha}}(V_{1}),\operatorname{Res}^{H_{\alpha_{0}}}_{H_{\alpha}}(V_{2}))\to{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Ext}}}^{i}_{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\mathbb{H})}(\operatorname{Res}^{\mathbb{H}}_{H_{\alpha}}(V_{1}),\operatorname{Res}^{\mathbb{H}}_{H_{\alpha}}(V_{2}))

is an isomorphism. However, the latter is the case for any pro-algebraic group.

∎

2.1.10.

From Proposition
2.1.9
we obtain:

Corollary 2.1.11
.

For
\mathbb{H}
being
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}}
,
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
or
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0}
, the operations

{\mathbf{C}}\mapsto{\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbb{H}}\text{ and }{\mathbf{D}}\mapsto\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\mathbb{H})}{\otimes}{\mathbf{D}}

define mutually inverse equivalences

{\operatorname{QCoh}}(H)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}\leftrightarrow{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(H)\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

2.2.
Sheaf theories

2.2.1.

Let
k
be a ground field, and let
\operatorname{Sch}_{k}
be the category of separated schemes of finite type over
k
.
Denote
\operatorname{pt}:=\operatorname{Spec}(k)
.

Let
\operatorname{Corr}(\operatorname{Sch}_{k})
be the category of correspondences on
\operatorname{Sch}_{k}
(see
[
GRV
, Sect. 2.1]
), and
let

\operatorname{Shv}(-):\operatorname{Corr}(\operatorname{Sch}_{k})\to\operatorname{DGCat}

be a sheaf theory in the sense of
[
GRV
, Sect. 2.2]
, equipped with a
right-lax
symmetric monoidal structure.

Informally, this means:

•

For every
X\in\operatorname{Sch}_{k}
we have an object
\operatorname{Shv}(X)\in\operatorname{DGCat}
;

•

For every morphism
f:X_{1}\to X_{2}
we have functors

f_{*}:\operatorname{Shv}(X_{1})\to\operatorname{Shv}(X_{2})\text{ and }f^{!}:\operatorname{Shv}(X_{2})\to\operatorname{Shv}(X_{1})

that satisfy base change for fiber squares;

•

We have a functor
\operatorname{Shv}(X_{1})\boxtimes\operatorname{Shv}(X_{2})\overset{\boxtimes}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}(X_{1}\times X_{2})
;

•

A homotopy-coherent data of compatibility between the above pieces of structure.

2.2.2.

Note that the symmetric monoidal structure on
\operatorname{Shv}(-)
endows
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt})
with a structure
of symmetric monoidal DG category. Moreover, the functor
\operatorname{Shv}(-)
naturally factors via

\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
.

We will make the following additional assumptions (cf.
[
GRV
, Sect. 4.1.1]
):

(1)

For every
X\in\operatorname{Sch}_{k}
, the category
\operatorname{Shv}(X)
is generated by objects that are

totally compact
3
3

3

Recall that for a monoidal DG category
{\mathbf{A}}
and
{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathbf{A}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, an object
m\in{\mathcal{M}}
is said to be totally
compact with respect to
{\mathbf{A}}
if the functor
\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{M}},{\mathbf{A}}}(m,-):{\mathcal{M}}\to{\mathbf{A}}
(of inner Hom relative to
{\mathbf{A}}
)
is a map of
{\mathbf{A}}
-module categories, see
[
GRV
, Sect. B.4]
.

with respect to
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt})
;

(2)

For every
X\in\operatorname{Sch}_{k}
, the map

\operatorname{Shv}(X)\otimes\operatorname{Shv}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt}),

induced by the unit of the canonical self-duality of
X\in\operatorname{Corr}(\operatorname{Sch}_{k})
,
is the counit of a self-duality of
\operatorname{Shv}(X)
as an object of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
.

Note that given condition (1), condition (2) can be reformulated as follows: there exists an anti self-equivalence

{\mathbb{D}}:(\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{tot.comp}})^{\operatorname{op}}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{tot.comp}},

such that for
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{tot.comp}}
and
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Shv}(X)
,

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(X),\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt})}({\mathcal{F}},{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime})\simeq(p_{X})_{*}\circ\Delta_{X}^{!}({\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{F}})\boxtimes{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}),

where:

•

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(X),\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt})}(-,-)
denotes the inner Hom in
\operatorname{Shv}(X)
relative to the action of
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt})
;

•

\Delta_{X}
denotes the diagonal map
X\to X\times X
;

•

p_{X}
denotes the projection
X\to\operatorname{pt}
.

Remark 2.2.3
.

In practice, the symmetric monoidal category
\operatorname{Shv}(\operatorname{pt})
is often semi-rigid
(or even rigid). In this case “totally compact” is the same as just “compact”.

2.2.4.

Let
\Phi:\operatorname{Shv}_{1}(-)\to\operatorname{Shv}_{2}(-)
be a natural transformation between two sheaf theories satisfying
the above assumptions.

Assume that for every
X
, the functor

\Phi_{X}:\operatorname{Shv}_{1}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}_{2}(X)

maps objects that are totally compact with respect to
\operatorname{Shv}_{1}(\operatorname{pt})
to
objects that are totally compact with respect to
\operatorname{Shv}_{2}(\operatorname{pt})
.

Note that in this case, for
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}_{1}(X)^{\operatorname{tot.comp}}
we have a tautologically defined map

(2.3)

{\mathbb{D}}(\Phi({\mathcal{F}}))\to\Phi({\mathbb{D}}({\mathcal{F}})).

We observe:

Proposition 2.2.5
.

Assume that the natural transformation (
2.3
) is an isomorphism. Then
the functor

\operatorname{Shv}_{2}(\operatorname{pt})\underset{\operatorname{Shv}_{1}(\operatorname{pt})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{1}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}_{2}(X)

is fully faithful.

2.3.
Proof of Proposition
2.2.5

2.3.1.

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a monoidal category; let
{\mathcal{M}}
be a dualizable
{\mathbf{A}}
-module category, and let

m\in{\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{tot.comp}}
. Denote by
{\mathbb{D}}(m)
the object of
{\mathcal{M}}^{\vee}
so that

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{M}},{\mathbf{A}}}(m,m^{\prime})=\langle m^{\prime},{\mathbb{D}}(m)\rangle,\quad m^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{M}},

where:

•

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{M}},{\mathbf{A}}}(-,-)
denotes the inner Hom in
{\mathcal{M}}
relative to
{\mathbf{A}}
;

•

{\mathcal{M}}^{\vee}\in{\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
denotes the dual of
{\mathcal{M}}
;

•

\langle-,-\rangle
denotes the pairing
{\mathcal{M}}\otimes{\mathcal{M}}^{\vee}\to{\mathbf{A}}
.

2.3.2.

Let now
\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}:{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}
be a homomorphism. Then the object

{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}}\otimes m\in{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\underset{{\mathbf{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}

is totally compact and we have

{\mathbb{D}}({\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}}\otimes m)\simeq{\mathbb{D}}(m)\otimes{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}}

as objects of

{\mathcal{M}}^{\vee}\underset{{\mathbf{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\simeq({\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\underset{{\mathbf{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}})^{\vee}.

2.3.3.

Let now
{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
be an object of
{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
, and let us be given a functor

\Phi_{\mathcal{M}}:{\mathcal{M}}\to{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}

as
{\mathbf{A}}
-module categories.

Suppose that
{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}
is also dualizable, and let us be given a functor

\Phi_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\vee}}:{\mathcal{M}}^{\vee}\to{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}{}^{\vee}

of
{\mathbf{A}}^{\operatorname{rev}}
-module categories, so that the diagram

(2.4)

\begin{CD}{\mathcal{M}}\otimes{\mathcal{M}}^{\vee}@>{\langle-,-\rangle}>{}>{\mathbf{A}}\\
@V{\Phi_{{\mathcal{M}}}\otimes\Phi_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\vee}}}V{}V@V{}V{\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}}V\\
{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}\otimes{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}{}^{\vee}@>{\langle-,-\rangle}>{}>{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\end{CD}

commutes.

Suppose that
\Phi_{\mathcal{M}}
maps totally compact objects to totally compact objects. From
(
2.4
) we obtain a map

(2.5)

{\mathbb{D}}(\Phi_{{\mathcal{M}}}(m))\to\Phi_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\vee}}({\mathbb{D}}(m)),\quad m\in{\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{tot.comp}}.

2.3.4.

We claim:

Proposition 2.3.5
.

Suppose that
{\mathcal{M}}
is generated by totally compact objects and suppose that
the natural transformation (
2.5
) is an isomorphism.
Then the induced functor

\Phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{M}}}:{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\underset{{\mathbf{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}\to{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}

is fully faithful.

It is clear that the assertion of Proposition
2.2.5
is a particular case of that of Proposition
2.3.5
.

2.3.6.
Proof of Proposition
2.3.5

It suffices to show that for
m\in{\mathcal{M}}^{\operatorname{tot.comp}}
and
m^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\underset{{\mathbf{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}
, the map

(2.6)

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\underset{{\mathbf{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}},{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}}({\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}}\otimes m,m^{\prime})\to\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}}(\Phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{M}}}({\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}}\otimes m),\Phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{M}}}(m^{\prime}))\simeq\\
\simeq\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime},{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}}(\Phi_{\mathcal{M}}(m),\Phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{M}}}(m^{\prime}))

is an isomorphism.

By Sect.
2.3.2
, we rewrite the above map as

(2.7)

\langle m^{\prime},{\mathbb{D}}(m)\otimes{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}}\rangle\to\langle\Phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{M}}}(m^{\prime}),{\mathbb{D}}(\Phi_{\mathcal{M}}(m))\rangle.

To prove that (
2.7
) is an isomorphism, we can take
m^{\prime}
of the form
{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}}\otimes m_{1}
for
m_{1}\in{\mathcal{M}}
.
In this case, the map (
2.7
) becomes

(2.8)

\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}(\langle m_{1},{\mathbb{D}}(m)\rangle)\to\langle\Phi_{\mathcal{M}}(m_{1}),{\mathbb{D}}(\Phi_{\mathcal{M}}(m))\rangle.

When we compose the latter map with (the isomorphism) (
2.5
) we obtain a map

(2.9)

\Phi_{\mathbf{A}}(\langle m_{1},{\mathbb{D}}(m)\rangle)\to\langle\Phi^{\prime}_{{\mathcal{M}}}(m_{1}),\Phi_{{\mathcal{M}}^{\vee}}({\mathbb{D}}(m))\rangle.

However, unwinding, we obtain that the map (
2.9
) is the isomorphism that expresses
the commutativity of (
2.4
).

∎[ Proposition
2.3.5
]

2.4.
Weil sheaves

2.4.1.

Let
X_{0}
be a scheme of finite type over
{\mathbb{F}}_{q}
, and let
X
be its base change to
\overline{{\mathbb{F}}}_{q}
. Let

\operatorname{Shv}(X)
denote the category of
\ell
-adic sheaves on
X
, as defined in
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 1.1]
.

By construction,

\operatorname{Shv}(X)\simeq\operatorname{Ind}(\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{constr}}).

The category
\operatorname{Shv}(X)
carries a canonical t-structure, whose heart is the category
\operatorname{Ind}(\operatorname{Perv}(X))
,
where
\operatorname{Perv}(X)
is the abelian category of perverse sheaves on
X
, as defined in
[
BBD
]
.

Recall (see
[
AGKRRV
, Theorem 1.1.6]
) that
\operatorname{Shv}(X)
is left-complete in its t-structure.

2.4.2.

Let
\operatorname{Frob}
denote the geometric Frobenius acting on
X
. Consider the resulting automorphism

\operatorname{Frob}^{*}
of
\operatorname{Shv}(X)
.

Define
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{weak-Weil}}(X)
to be the category of pairs
({\mathcal{F}},\alpha)
, where
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(X)

and
\alpha
is a map
\operatorname{Frob}^{*}({\mathcal{F}})\to{\mathcal{F}}
, which by adjunction is the same as a map

(2.10)

{\mathcal{F}}\to\operatorname{Frob}_{*}({\mathcal{F}}).

We have a monadic adjunction

{\mathbf{ind}}_{\operatorname{weak-Weil}}:\operatorname{Shv}(X)\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{weak-Weil}}(X):{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{weak-Weil}},

where
{\mathbf{ind}}_{\operatorname{weak-Weil}}
sends
{\mathcal{F}}
to

\underset{n\geq 0}{\oplus}\,(\operatorname{Frob}^{n})_{*}({\mathcal{F}}),

equipped with the natural weak Weil structure.

2.4.3.

Since the above monad on
\operatorname{Shv}(X)
is t-exact, we obtain that
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{weak-Weil}}(X)
carries
a unique t-structure, so that both functors
{\mathbf{ind}}_{\operatorname{weak-Weil}}
and
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{weak-Weil}}
are t-exact.

It follows formally that the category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{weak-Weil}}(X)
is also left-complete in its t-structure.

2.4.4.

Let

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{weak-Weil}}(X)

be the full subcategory consisting of objects
({\mathcal{F}},\alpha)
, for which
\alpha
is an isomorphism.
Since
\operatorname{Frob}
is an equivalence, this is the same as requiring that (
2.10
) be an isomorphism.

We have a localization

\jmath^{*}:\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{weak-Weil}}(X)\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X):\jmath_{*},

where
\jmath^{*}
sends

({\mathcal{F}},\alpha)\mapsto\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,(\operatorname{Frob}^{n})_{*}({\mathcal{F}}),

with the transition maps induced by
\alpha
, and where the colimit is equipped with a natural
Frobenius-equivariant structure.

Denote

{\mathbf{ind}}_{\operatorname{Weil}}=\jmath^{*}\circ{\mathbf{ind}}_{\operatorname{weak-Weil}}\text{ and }{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Weil}}:={\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{weak-Weil}}\circ\jmath_{*}.

The same logic as in Sect.
2.4.3
applies, and we obtain that
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)
carries a unique t-structure,
for which the functors
{\mathbf{ind}}_{\operatorname{Weil}}
and
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Weil}}
are t-exact. Moreover,
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)
is left-complete
in its t-structure.

2.4.5.

Set

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)^{\operatorname{constr}}:=\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)\underset{\operatorname{Shv}(X)}{\times}\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{constr}}.

Define

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X):=\operatorname{Ind}(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)^{\operatorname{constr}}).

We have a tautologically defined functor:

(2.11)

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X).

By a slight abuse of notation, we will denote by the same symbol
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Weil}}

the resulting functor

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}(X).

2.4.6.

We claim:

Proposition 2.4.7
.

(a)
The functor (
2.11
) preserves compactness;

(b)
The functor (
2.11
) is fully faithful;

Proof.

To prove point (a), it suffices to show that if an object
({\mathcal{F}},\alpha)\in\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)

is such that
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}(X)
is compact (i.e., lies in
\operatorname{Shv}(X)^{\operatorname{constr}}
), then this
object is compact. Since
(\jmath^{*},\jmath_{*})
is a localization, it suffices to prove the same assertion for

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{weak-Weil}}(X)
.

However, this follows from the fact that
({\mathcal{F}},\alpha)
can be written as

\operatorname{cofib}({\mathbf{ind}}_{\operatorname{weak-Weil}}(\operatorname{Frob}_{*}({\mathcal{F}}))\to{\mathbf{ind}}_{\operatorname{weak-Weil}}({\mathcal{F}})),

for the map obtained by the
({\mathbf{ind}}_{\operatorname{weak-Weil}},{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{weak-Weil}})
-adjunction from the map

\operatorname{Frob}_{*}({\mathcal{F}})\to\underset{n\geq 0}{\oplus}\,(\operatorname{Frob}^{n})_{*}({\mathcal{F}})={\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{weak-Weil}}\circ{\mathbf{ind}}_{\operatorname{weak-Weil}}({\mathcal{F}}).
