Point (b) follows formally from point (a), since the functor (
2.11
) is by definition fully faithful on compacts.

∎

2.4.8.

The inclusion

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)^{c}=\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)^{\operatorname{constr}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)

is stable under the truncations with respect to the t-structure. Hence
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)^{c}
inherits
a t-structure, which in turn induces a t-structure on
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)
.

By construction, the functor (
2.11
) is t-exact, and hence so is the functor

{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Weil}}:\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}(X).

Since the latter functor is conservative, an object of
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)
is connective/coconnective
if and only if its image under
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Weil}}
is connective/coconnective.

2.4.9.

We now introduce two more categories that we will use later.

One is:

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X):=\operatorname{Ind}(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)^{\operatorname{constr}}),

where

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)^{\operatorname{constr}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)^{\operatorname{constr}}

is a full subcategory that consists of objects, whose
perverse cohomologies have irreducible subquotients that are pure of some integral weight.

Note that for
X_{0}=\operatorname{pt}
, the category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)
is by definition
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})
.

By construction, the tautological functor

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)

preserves compactness and hence is fully faithful.

2.4.10.

The other is:

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X):=\operatorname{Ind}(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X)^{\operatorname{constr}}),

where

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X)^{\operatorname{constr}}\subset\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)^{\operatorname{constr}}

is a full subcategory that consists of objects, whose
perverse cohomologies have irreducible subquotients that are pure of weight
0
.

Note that for
X_{0}=\operatorname{pt}
, the category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X)
is by definition
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0})
.

By construction, the tautological functor

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)

preserves compactness and hence is fully faithful.

2.4.11.

The t-structure on
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)^{c}
induces t-structures on the (small) categories

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)^{\operatorname{constr}}\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X)^{\operatorname{constr}},

which in turn induce t-structures on

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X),

respectively.

By construction, the embeddings

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)

are t-exact.

2.5.
The category of relevant sheaves

2.5.1.

In the setting of Sect.
2.2.1
, take
k={\mathbb{F}}_{q}
. We will consider several sheaf theories associated to this situation.
In the notations of Sect.
2.4.1
, each sends
X_{0}\in\operatorname{Sch}_{/{\mathbb{F}}_{q}}
to the following category:

(1)

\operatorname{Shv}(X)
;

(2)

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)
;

(3)

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)
, which is the full subcategory of
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)
consisting of objects
{\mathcal{F}}
, such that for every
x\in|X|
,
the eigenvalues of
\operatorname{Frob}_{x}
acting on the *-stalks of irreducible subquotients of perverse cohomologies of
{\mathcal{F}}
are
q_{x}
-Weil numbers.

Evaluating on
X_{0}=\operatorname{pt}
, the above examples produce the categories

\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}},\,\,{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}})\text{ and }{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}),

respectively.

Remark 2.5.2
.

The above definition of
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)
is a variant of the (usual) category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,mxd}}(X)
, the difference being
that we require the eigenvalues to be Weil numbers as opposed to mock Weil numbers (see Sect.
2.1.2
for what this
means).

The fact that
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,mxd}}(X)
is a sheaf theory is a consequence of Weil-II (specifically, Theorems 3.3.1 and 6.1.2
in
[
De2
]
).

The fact that
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)
is a sheaf theory is obtained by combining
[
De1
, Theorem 5.2.2]

(stability under !-puhforwards) and the “de finitude” trick of
[
De2
, Sects. 6.1.4-6.1.10]
(stability under *-puhforwards).

That said, the categories
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,mxd}}(X)
and
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)
are expressible through one another.
Namely, the proof of Theorem
2.5.8
below shows that the natural functor

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,mxd}})\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,mxd}}(X)

is an equivalence, where
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,mxd}}
is a variant of
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})
, where we replace “Weil” by “mock Weil”.

2.5.3.

Note also that we have the natural transformations

\operatorname{Shv}(-)^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(-)^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}\to\operatorname{Shv}(-)

satisfying the assumptions of Proposition
2.2.5
.

Hence, we obtain the fully faithful functors

(2.12)

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}})\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X),

(2.13)

\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}(X),

and

(2.14)

\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}(X).

2.5.4.

Let

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}(X)

denote the essential image of the functor (
2.13
).

We claim:

Lemma 2.5.5
.

The category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
is compactly generated by irreducible
objects
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Perv}(X)
for which there exists an integer
n
such that
(\operatorname{Frob}^{n})^{*}({\mathcal{F}})\simeq{\mathcal{F}}
.

Proof.

By construction,
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
is generated by images under
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Weil}}
of irreducible
objects in
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)^{\heartsuit}
, i.e., irreducible Weil perverse sheaves. It is easy to
see that any such is of the form

(2.15)

{\mathcal{F}}\oplus\operatorname{Frob}^{*}({\mathcal{F}})\oplus...\oplus(\operatorname{Frob}^{n-1})^{*}({\mathcal{F}}),

where
{\mathcal{F}}
is an irreducible object in
\operatorname{Perv}(X)
such that
(\operatorname{Frob}^{n})^{*}({\mathcal{F}})\simeq{\mathcal{F}}
,
while
(\operatorname{Frob}^{m})^{*}({\mathcal{F}})\not\simeq{\mathcal{F}}
for
m<n
.

∎

Corollary 2.5.6
.

(a)
The inclusion
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Shv}(X)
is compatible with the t-structure on
\operatorname{Shv}(X)
.

(b)
The induced t-structure on
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
preserves the subcategory of compact objects.

(c) The irreducible objects in
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)^{\heartsuit}
are those described in
Lemma
2.5.5
.

2.5.7.

We claim:

Theorem 2.5.8
.

The functor (
2.12
) is an equivalence.

Proof.

We need to show that the essential image of the functor (
2.12
) generates the target
category. Hence, it suffices to show that every object
{\mathcal{F}}\in(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X))^{\heartsuit}

lies in the essential image of (
2.12
).

We can assume that
{\mathcal{F}}
is
irreducible. We will show that in this case
{\mathcal{F}}
has the form

{\mathcal{F}}^{0}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}},

where
{\mathcal{F}}^{0}\in(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))^{\heartsuit}
and
{\mathfrak{l}}
is a line in
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(\operatorname{pt})
.

Any irreducible object of
(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X))^{\heartsuit}
is a Goresky-MacPherson extension
of an irreducible
lisse
object on a locally-closed smooth subscheme of
X
. Since the operation of Goresky-MacPherson
extension preserves weights, this allows us to assume that
{\mathcal{F}}
is lisse.

Now, the desired result follows from
[
LLaf
, Corollary VII.8]
4
4

4

With the correction in
[
De3
, Sect. 1.7-1.9]
.
.

∎

Corollary 2.5.9
.

The essential image of the functor (
2.14
) equals
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
.

2.5.10.

From Corollaries
2.5.9
and
2.1.11
we obtain:

Corollary 2.5.11
.

The category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
upgrades to an object of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}
so that

(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X))^{{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X),

as objects of
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
.

2.6.
Compatibility of t-structures

2.6.1.

Let
{\mathbf{A}}
be a monoidal category equipped with a t-structure so that the monoidal operation is right t-exact,
and
{\mathbf{1}}_{\mathbf{A}}
is connective. Let
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
be right and left
{\mathbf{A}}
-module categories each equipped with a t-structure,
and such that the action functors are right t-exact.

In this case we equip

{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\underset{{\mathbf{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}_{2}

with a t-structure by declaring that the connective subcategory is generated under colimits
by the image of connective objects under

{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\otimes{\mathcal{M}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\underset{{\mathbf{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}_{2}.

We call the resulting t-structure on
{\mathcal{M}}_{1}\underset{{\mathbf{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}_{2}
the
tensor product
t-structure.

2.6.2.

We will now consider a paradigm in which the tensor product t-structure is particularly well-behaved.
Let
{\mathbf{A}}
and
{\mathcal{M}}\in{\mathbf{A}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}
be as above. Let
{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}
be another monoidal category equipped with a
t-structure as above. Let
\Phi:{\mathbf{A}}\to{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}
be a right t-exact monoidal functor. We equip

{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}:={\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\underset{{\mathbf{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}

with a t-structure by the above recipe.

Assume that
\Phi
admits a continuous right adjoint. Assume that:

•

The natural structure on
\Phi^{R}
of right-lax compatibility with the
{\mathbf{A}}
-bimodule structure is strict;

•

The functor
\Phi^{R}
is conservative;

•

The functor
\Phi^{R}
is t-exact;

•

The action of
\Phi^{R}({\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}})
on
{\mathcal{M}}
is t-exact.

Under these circumstances, we claim:

Lemma 2.6.3
.

Both functors in the adjunction

(2.16)

{\mathcal{M}}\simeq{\mathbf{A}}\underset{{\mathbf{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}\overset{\Phi\otimes\operatorname{Id}}{\underset{\Phi^{R}\otimes\operatorname{Id}}{\rightleftarrows}}{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}\underset{{\mathbf{A}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{M}}=:{\mathcal{M}}^{\prime}

are t-exact, and the right adjoint is conservative.

Proof.

The fact that
\Phi^{R}
is conservative is equivalent to the fact that the essential image of
\Phi
generates the target category,
which implies that the essential image of
\Phi\otimes\operatorname{Id}
generates the target category, which in turn implies that

\Phi^{R}\otimes\operatorname{Id}
is conservative.

The left adjoint in (
2.16
) is right t-exact by construction. Hence, the right adjoint in (
2.16
) is left t-exact
(by adjunction). However, it is also right t-exact, since
\Phi^{R}
was assumed to be right t-exact.

Under these circumstances,
the left adjoint is t-exact if and only if the resulting monad is t-exact. However, this monad given by the action of
\Phi^{R}({\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}})
on
{\mathcal{M}}
.
Since this action it t-exact, this implies the assertion of the lemma.

∎

2.6.4.

We apply Lemma
2.6.3
to

{\mathbf{A}}:={\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}}),\,\,{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}:=\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\text{ and }{\mathcal{M}}:=\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X).

We obtain that the category

\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)

acquires a t-structure, so that the adjoint functors

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)

are both t-exact.

2.6.5.

Recall now (see Corollary
2.5.6
) that the category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
carries a t-structure, induced by that on
\operatorname{Shv}(X)
.
We claim:

Proposition 2.6.6
.

The equivalence

\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)\overset{\sim}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)

is t-exact.

Proof.

The fact that the functor
\to
is right t-exact is immediate. To show that it is t-exact, it is sufficient to
show that its inverse is right t-exact.

Since the t-structure on
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
is obtained by extending the t-structure on

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)^{c}
, it suffices to show that the inverse functor sends irreducible objects
in
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)^{\heartsuit}
to objects in the heart in the left-hand side.

According to Corollary
2.5.6
, irreducible objects in
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)^{\heartsuit}
are irrreducible
objects in
\operatorname{Shv}(X)
that are invariant under some power of the Frobenius. Such an object is direct
summand of an object (
2.15
), so it is enough to show that the latter object gets sent to
an object in the heart in the left-hand side. However, (
2.15
) is the image of an object
in
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)^{\heartsuit}
under

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)\to\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}(X),

while the composite functor is t-exact.
∎

Remark 2.6.7
.

We can also apply Lemma
2.6.3
to

{\mathbf{A}}:={\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}),\,\,{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}:={\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}}),\,\,{\mathcal{M}}:=\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X),

and obtain a t-structure on

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}})\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X).

As in Proposition
2.6.6
, one shows that the equivalence (
2.12
) is t-exact.

Remark 2.6.8
.

Furthermore, we can take

{\mathbf{A}}:={\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}),\,\,{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}:=\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\text{ and }{\mathcal{M}}:=\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X),

and produce a t-structure on

\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X).

It follows, however, that this is the same t-structure as the one arising from the equivalence

\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\simeq\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\simeq\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X).

2.6.9.

We now claim:

Theorem 2.6.10
.

(a) The category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
is left-complete with respect to its t-structure.

(b) The category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)
is left-complete with respect to its t-structure.

Proof.

Point (a) of the theorem follows by repeating the argument of
[
AGKRRV
, Theorem 1.1.6]
5
5

5

Note that Beilinson’s
theorem, used in
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. E.1]
, is equally applicable to
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
.
.

We now prove point (b). Note that by Corollary
2.1.11
, we can regard
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
as a category acted on by

{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}}
so that

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)\simeq(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X))^{{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}}}.

In other words,
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)
is the totalization of the cosimplicial category with terms

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}})^{\otimes n}

and t-exact face maps. Since all terms are left-complete (e.g., by
[
AGKRRV
, Lemma 2.1.3(a)]
), we obtain that

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)
is also left-complete.

∎

2.6.11.

From point (a) of Theorem
2.6.10
, we obtain:

Corollary 2.6.12
.

An object of
\operatorname{Shv}(X)
belongs to
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
if and only if all irreducible subquotients of its perverse cohomologies
are invariant under some power of the Frobenius.

Remark 2.6.13
.

An assertion parallel to Theorem
2.6.10
(b) holds for
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)
: in the proof one uses

{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
instead of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}}
and Corollary
2.5.11
.

2.7.
A semi-simple category of sheaves

2.7.1.

Denote

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X):=\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X).

We equip
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)
with a t-structure by the recipe of Sect.
2.6.1
.

2.7.2.

Note that we have a naturally defined forgetful functor

(2.17)

\phi:\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X),

but it is
not
an equivalence.

2.7.3.

Recall that a DG category
{\mathbf{C}}
equipped with a t-structure is said to be
semi-simple
if:

•

{\mathbf{C}}^{\heartsuit}
is a semi-simple abelian category;

•

The functor
D({\mathbf{C}}^{\heartsuit})\to{\mathbf{C}}
is an equivalence.

2.7.4.

We claim:

Proposition 2.7.5
.

(a) The category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)
is semi-simple.

(b) Its irreducible objects are the direct summands of
images under the tautological functor

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)

of objects (
2.15
), where
{\mathcal{F}}
is an irreducible perverse sheaf with a Weil structure

over

{\mathbb{F}}_{q^{n}}
that is pure of weight
0
. Each such direct summand is of the form

(\operatorname{Frob}^{i})^{*}({\mathcal{F}})
for
0\leq i<n
.

Remark 2.7.6
.

Note that although the functor
\phi
of (
2.17
) is not an equivalence,
it induces a bijection on
semi-simple objects: indeed, it follows from Corollary
2.5.6
(c) and the proof of
Theorem
2.5.8
that any irreducible perverse sheaf
in
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
lies in the image of
\phi
.

In particular, the functor
\phi
is t-exact.

2.7.7.

Before we prove the proposition, we give a criterion for semi-simplicity:

Lemma 2.7.8
.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be a DG category equipped with a t-structure. Assume that
{\mathbf{C}}
is compactly generated
by a collection of objects
{\mathbf{c}}_{\alpha}\in{\mathbf{C}}^{\heartsuit}
such that:

(i)
{\mathbf{c}}_{\alpha}
are semi-simple as objects of
{\mathbf{C}}^{\heartsuit}
;

(ii) For any
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}
, the object
{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\mathbf{C}}({\mathbf{c}}_{\alpha_{1}},{\mathbf{c}}_{\alpha_{2}})
is acyclic off degree
0
.

Then
{\mathbf{C}}
is semi-simple.

2.7.9.
Proof of Proposition
2.7.5
, Step 1

It suffices to show that for a pair of irreducible objects
{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X))^{\heartsuit}
, the object

(2.18)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)}({\mathbf{1}}_{\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}}\otimes{\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathbf{1}}_{\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}}\otimes{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\in\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}

is acyclic off degree
0
.

Note that the object (
2.18
) is obtained by applying the forgetful functor
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0})\to\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}

to

(2.19)

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X),{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0})}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0}).

Hence, it suffices to show that (
2.19
) lies in the heart of the t-structure.

2.7.10.
Proof of Proposition
2.7.5
, Step 2

In its turn, the object (
2.19
)
is obtained from

(2.20)

\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X),{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{loc.fin}}.}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\in{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{loc.fin}}.

by applying the right adjoint to the embedding

(2.21)

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0})\hookrightarrow{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}})={\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{loc.fin}}.

We will show that this right adjoint annihilates
H^{i}(\underline{\operatorname{Hom}}_{\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X),{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{loc.fin}}.}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}))

for
i>0
and acts as identity for
i=0
.

2.7.11.
Proof of Proposition
2.7.5
, Step 3

Note that the object of
\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}
underlying (
2.20
) is

(2.22)

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(X)}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}),

with the action of
1\in{\mathbb{Z}}
given by the Weil structure.

Now, by
[
BBD
]
, the generalized eigenvalues of the Frobenius on

H^{i}({\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(X)}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}))\simeq\operatorname{Ext}^{i}_{\operatorname{Shv}(X)}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})

are Weil numbers of weights
\geq i
.

Hence, all
H^{i}({\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(X)}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}))
with
i>0
are annihilated by the right
adjoint of (
2.21
).

2.7.12.
Proof of Proposition
2.7.5
, Step 4

Finally, for
i=0
,

(2.23)

H^{0}({\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(X)}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}))\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Shv}(X)}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}),

and let us consider two cases:

Case 1:

{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Weil}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1})
and
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Weil}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})
does not
have common isotypic components. In this case
\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Shv}(X)}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})=0

and there is nothing to prove,

Case 2:

{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Weil}}({\mathcal{F}}_{1})
and
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Weil}}({\mathcal{F}}_{2})
do
have common isotypic components. Then by the description of the irreducible
objects in
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)
(see the proof of Lemma
2.5.5
), we have

{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\simeq{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\otimes{\mathfrak{l}},

where
{\mathfrak{l}}
is a line in
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0})
, and

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Shv}(X)}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})
is isomorphic to
{\mathfrak{l}}^{\oplus n}
for some
n
.
Hence, (
2.23
) already belongs to
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0})
, and hence, the right
adjoint of (
2.21
) applied to
H^{0}({\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(X)}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}))

acts as identity.

∎[Proposition
2.7.5
]

Remark 2.7.13
.

As in Theorem
2.6.10
(b), from Proposition
2.7.5
one deduces that the category

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X)
is left-complete in its t-structure.

2.8.
A contraction on the category of sheaves

2.8.1.

We are now ready to carry out the key construction of this section:

Theorem-Construction 2.8.2
.

The DG category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
canonically lifts to an object of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}
. Moreover:

(i) The
attracting category
corresponding to this coaction is
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)
.

(ii) The resulting functors

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\overset{\psi}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)\overset{\phi}{\to}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)

are compatible with the coactions of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})
, where the coaction
on
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)
is via
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0}
.

Remark 2.8.3
.

Note that as a consequence of Theorem
2.8.2
we obtain that the forgetful functor
\phi

of (
2.17
) admits a retraction, to be denoted

\psi:\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X).

In view of Proposition
2.7.5
, we obtain that on the category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
there is a
well-defined functor of
semi-simplification
.

2.8.4.

The rest of this subsection is devoted to the proof of Theorem
2.8.2
.

2.8.5.

Recall that according to Corollary
2.5.9
, we have

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\simeq\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X).

Set
6
6

6

The reason for the choice of notation is that the role of
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X)
vis-a-vis
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
is
similar to that of
A{\operatorname{-mod}}^{\operatorname{gr}}
vis-a-vis
A{\operatorname{-mod}}
, for a
{\mathbb{Z}}
-graded algebra
A
.

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X):={\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X),

where

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})\to{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})

is given by restriction along the canonical homomorphism
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}

(see Sect.
2.1.4
).

Remark 2.8.6
.

The category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X)
has been introduced and studied in
[
HL
]
.

2.8.7.

Thus,

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\simeq\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X).

According to Theorem
1.1.7
, in order to prove Theorem
2.8.2
, we need to lift

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X)
to an object of
{\mathbb{Z}}\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}^{\operatorname{Filtr}}
, so that the corresponding category

(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X))_{0}
identifies with
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)
.

2.8.8.

Note that the short exact sequence

1\to{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}\to{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0}\to 1

leads to an equivalence

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})\simeq\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0})}{\otimes}{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}).

Hence, we can rewrite

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X)\simeq\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X).

We will lift
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)
to an object of
\operatorname{DGCat}^{\operatorname{Filtr}}
in a way compatible with the action of

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})
and such that

(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{0}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X).

This will induce the required structure on
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X)
.

2.8.9.

We let

(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq n}\subset\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)

be the full subcategory generated by

(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))^{\operatorname{constr}}_{\leq n}\subset(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))^{\operatorname{constr}},

where the latter is the subcategory consisting of objects, all of whose perverse cohomologies
have weights
\leq n
.

Warning
: note that
(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq n}
is
not
what is called
“mixed complexes of weight
\leq n
” in
[
BBD
]
.

2.8.10.

By construction, the category
(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq n}
is compactly generated, and the embedding

(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq n}\hookrightarrow(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq n+1}

preserves compactness (and, hence, admits a colimit-preserving right adjoint).

Furthermore, it is clear that

\underset{n}{\operatorname{colim}}\,(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq n}\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)

is an equivalence.

2.8.11.

It remains to identify

(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq 0}/(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq-1}\simeq\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X).

We will think of
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X)
as a full subcategory of
(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq 0}
.
We will show that as such it identifies with the right orthogonal

(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq-1}^{\perp}\subset(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq 0}.

2.8.12.

First, we claim that for
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}\in(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq-1}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\in\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X)
,
we have

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})=0.

To prove this, we can assume that
{\mathcal{F}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
are constructible and perverse. We have

{\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})={\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2})\simeq({\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(X)}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}))^{\operatorname{Frob}}.

Now,
H^{i}({\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(X)}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}))=0
for
i<0
and for
i\geq 0
the Frobenius eigenvalues
on it have weights
\geq i+1
. This implies that

\left(H^{i}({\mathop{\operatorname{{\mathcal{H}}\it om}}}_{\operatorname{Shv}(X)}({\mathcal{F}}_{1},{\mathcal{F}}_{2}))\right)^{\operatorname{Frob}}=0,

as desired.

2.8.13.

Thus, we obtain a (fully faithful) inclusion

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X)\subset(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq-1}^{\perp}.

It remains to show that the essential image of this inclusion generates the target. This is equivalent to showing
that the essential image of

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X)\hookrightarrow(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq 0}\to(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq 0}/(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq-1}

generates the target.

However, this is obvious: the right-hand side is generated by perverse sheaves of weights
\leq 0
, while perverse sheaves of weights

\leq-1
go to zero.

∎[Theorem
2.8.2
]

Remark 2.8.14
.

Recall that the category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)
is left-complete in its t-structure (see Remark
2.6.13
).
We claim that an object of
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)
belongs to
(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq n}
if (and only if) all of its
perverse cohomologies do (in particular,
(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq n}
is also left-complete in its t-structure).

Indeed, note that
(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{>n}
also acquires a t-structure, such that the projection

(j_{>n})^{L}:\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\to(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{>n}

is t-exact.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)
be such that all of its perverse cohomologies have weights
\leq n
.
We wish to show that
{\mathcal{F}}\in(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq n}
, i.e., that
(j_{>n})^{L}({\mathcal{F}})=0
.
Now, by assumption all of the cohomologies of
(j_{>n})^{L}({\mathcal{F}})
vanish. Hence, it suffices to show
that the t-structure on
(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{>n}
is separated. We claim that
(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{>n}

is in fact left-complete in its t-structure:

Note that the functor

j_{>n}:(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{>n}\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)

is also t-exact. Since
j_{>n}
commutes with limits, it follows formally that
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{>n}
is
left-complete in its t-structure.

Remark 2.8.15
.

Applying Lemma
2.6.3
to

{\mathbf{A}}:={\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0}),\,\,{\mathbf{A}}^{\prime}:=\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\text{ and }{\mathcal{M}}:=\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)

we obtain that the category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X)
also acquires a t-structure so that the adjoint
functors

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\rightleftarrows\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X)

are t-exact. Since
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)
is left-complete in its t-structure, the same is true
for
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X)
.

Furthermore, the categories

(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X))_{\leq n}\simeq\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0})}{\otimes}(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{\leq n}

and

(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X))_{>n}\simeq\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0})}{\otimes}(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))_{>n}

also acquire t-structures, in which they are left-complete, and the functors
(i_{\leq n},i_{\leq n}^{R})
and
(j_{\geq n}^{L},j_{\geq n})

are t-exact.

2.9.
The t-exactness property of the contraction

2.9.1.

We claim:

Proposition 2.9.2
.

The coaction functor

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})

is t-exact.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.6.3
, it suffices to show that the coaction functor

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m}),

is t-exact.

It suffices to show that the above functor sends objects in the heart to objects in the heart.
By construction, this functor send
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)
to the object in

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1}/{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\simeq\operatorname{Filtr}(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))=\operatorname{Funct}({\mathbb{Z}},\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))

given by

n\mapsto{\mathcal{F}}_{n}:=i_{\leq n}\circ(i_{\leq n})^{R}({\mathcal{F}}).

However, the latter is the canonical weight filtration on
{\mathcal{F}}
.

∎

2.9.3.

We now claim:

Proposition 2.9.4
.

The contraction functor

\psi:\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)

is t-exact.

Proof.

It suffices to show that the functor

\psi_{0}:\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)

is t-exact.

Using Lemma
2.6.3
, this is equivalent to showing that the corresponding functor

\psi_{0}:\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X)

is t-exact.

To prove this, it suffices to show that the functor
\psi_{0}
sends irreducible perverse sheaves in
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)

to objects in the heart.

Let
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)
be pure of weight
n
. If
n>0
, then it is annihilated by the functor
(i_{\leq 0})^{R}

and hence by
\psi_{0}
.

If
n<0
, then the functor
(i_{\leq 0})^{R}
sends it to itself, but it is annihilated by the projection

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)_{\leq 0}\to\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)_{\leq 0}/\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)_{\leq-1}.

Finally, when
n=0
, then
\psi_{0}({\mathcal{F}})={\mathcal{F}}
.

∎

3.
A contraction on the stack of local systems

In this section we will apply Theorem-Construction
2.8.2
to obtain a contraction of the
stack of arithmetic local systems onto the locus, where the underlying geometric local system is semi-simple.

This contraction will allow us to prove our main result about the excursion algebra, Theorem
3.7.2
.

3.1.
Weil sheaves with prescribed singular support

3.1.1.

Let
{\mathcal{N}}\subset T^{*}(X)
be a conical Zariski-closed subset. Let

\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}(X)

be the full subcategory defined as in
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. E.4]
.

Let

(3.1)

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil}}_{\mathcal{N}}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X),\,\,\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}_{\mathcal{N}}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X),

(3.2)

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}_{\mathcal{N}}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X),\,\,\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,0}}_{\mathcal{N}}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,0}}(X)

be the full subcategories equal to the preimage(s) of
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}(X)
under the respective forgetful functors to
\operatorname{Shv}(X)
.

Note that the above subcategories are submodule categories for the actions of

{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}},\,\,{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{loc.fin}}={\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}}),\,\,{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{wt}}={\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})\text{ and }{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}={\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0}),

respectively.

3.1.2.

Set also

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}_{\mathcal{N}}(X):=\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\underset{\operatorname{Shv}(X)}{\times}\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}(X)

and

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}_{\mathcal{N}}(X):=\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)\underset{\operatorname{Shv}(X)}{\times}\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}(X).

Recall that we have

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)\simeq(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X))^{{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}}},\,\,\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\simeq(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X))^{{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}}

and also

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,0}}(X)\simeq(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X))^{{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0}}.

3.1.3.

We claim:

Lemma 3.1.4
.

The subcategories

(3.3)

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}_{\mathcal{N}}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}_{\mathcal{N}}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)

are stable under the coactions of

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}}),\,\,{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})\text{ and }{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0}),

respectively. Moreover, we have the equalities:

(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}_{\mathcal{N}}(X))^{{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}}}=\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}_{\mathcal{N}}(X),\,\,(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}_{\mathcal{N}}(X))^{{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}}=\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}_{\mathcal{N}}(X)

and

(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}_{\mathcal{N}}(X))^{{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0}}=\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}^{\operatorname{Weil,0}}(X)

as full subcategories of

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X),\,\,\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\text{ and }\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,0}}(X),

respectively.

Proof.

Consider the following general paradigm:

Let
\mathbb{H}
be as in Corollary
2.1.11
. Let
{\mathbf{C}}
be an object of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\mathbb{H})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{comod}
, and set

{\mathbf{D}}:={\mathbf{C}}^{\mathbb{H}}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\mathbb{H})\operatorname{-}\mathbf{mod}.

Consider the adjoint pair

{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\mathbb{H}}:{\mathbf{D}}\rightleftarrows{\mathbf{C}}:\operatorname{Av}^{\mathbb{H}}.

Let
{\mathbf{C}}_{1}\subset{\mathbf{C}}
be a full subcategory, stable under the comonad
{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\mathbb{H}}\circ\operatorname{Av}^{\mathbb{H}}
.
Set

{\mathbf{D}}_{1}:={\mathbf{D}}\underset{{\mathbf{C}}}{\times}{\mathbf{C}}_{1},

Then
{\mathbf{C}}_{1}
is a
{\operatorname{QCoh}}(\mathbb{H})
-comodule and

{\mathbf{C}}_{1}^{\mathbb{H}}={\mathbf{D}}_{1}.

We claim that the above paradigm is applicable in the situation of the lemma. I.e., we have to show that the
subcategories (
3.3
) are stable under the corresponding comonad. By definition, the question reduces
to one about irreducible objects in the heart of the t-structure.

Such an object is a direct summand of an object (
2.15
), which lies in the image of

{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\mathbb{H}}:{\mathbf{D}}\to{\mathbf{C}},

and an action of the comonad on it is given by tensoring with the algebra of regular functions on
\mathbb{H}
.
The latter operation tautologically preserves the singular support condition.

∎

3.1.5.

Thus, we obtain that the category
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}_{\mathcal{N}}(X)
is a comodule over
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})
, and in
particular over
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})
. Set

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}_{\mathcal{N}}(X):=(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}_{\mathcal{N}}(X))^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\subset(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X))^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}=\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X).

Note that under the identification

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X)\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\simeq\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X),

the full subcategory

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}_{\mathcal{N}}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X)

corresponds to

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)\simeq\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X).

3.1.6.

We now claim:

Proposition 3.1.7
.

The full subcategory
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}_{\mathcal{N}}(X)\subset\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
is stable under the coaction of

{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})
.

Proof.

The assertion of the proposition is equivalent to the statement that the endofunctors

i_{\leq n}\circ(i_{\leq n})^{R}
of
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)
preserve the subcategory
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}_{\mathcal{N}}(X)
.

Note that the functor
i_{\leq n}\circ(i_{\leq n})^{R}
is t-exact (it is enough to check this on the heart, where it is obvious).
Hence, it is sufficient to show that for an irreducible object
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}_{\mathcal{N}}(X)^{\heartsuit}
, the object

i_{\leq n}\circ(i_{\leq n})^{R}({\mathcal{F}})
belongs to
\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}_{\mathcal{N}}(X)
.

However, this is obvious:

If
\operatorname{wt}({\mathcal{F}})\leq n
, then
i_{\leq n}\circ(i_{\leq n})^{R}({\mathcal{F}})\simeq{\mathcal{F}}
, and if

\operatorname{wt}({\mathcal{F}})>n
, then
i_{\leq n}\circ(i_{\leq n})^{R}({\mathcal{F}})=0
.

∎

Remark 3.1.8
.

Note that we do not know in general whether the category
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}(X)
is compactly generated (or even dualizable).
Hence, we do not know this either for the categories
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}^{?}(X)
introduced above, with the exception of

\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}_{\mathcal{N}}(X)
, which
is semi-simple and hence compactly generated, and hence so is
\operatorname{Shv}_{\mathcal{N}}^{\operatorname{Weil,0}}(X)\simeq(\operatorname{Shv}^{\operatorname{relev},0}_{\mathcal{N}}(X))^{{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0}}
.

3.2.
Weil local systems

From now on we will assume that
X
is connected.

3.2.1.

We will apply the above discussion to
{\mathcal{N}}=\{0\}
. In this case will adopt a special notation

\operatorname{Shv}^{?}_{\{0\}}(X)=:\operatorname{QLisse}^{?}(X).

3.2.2.

Let us choose a base point
x\in X(\overline{k})
. This choice gives rise to a fiber functor

\operatorname{QLisse}(X)^{\heartsuit}\to\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}.

Denote by

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)

the corresponding pro-algebraic group.

Restricting to
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)^{\heartsuit}\subset\operatorname{QLisse}(X)^{\heartsuit}
we obtain a pro-algebraic group, to be denoted

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev}}(X),

which is naturally a quotient of
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)
.

Denote by

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red}}(X)\text{ and }\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red,relev}}(X)

the maximal reductive quotients of
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)
and
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev}}(X)
, respectively.

3.2.3.

We have tautologically defined t-exact functors

(3.4)

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X))\to\operatorname{QLisse}(X)\text{ and }{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev}}(X))\to\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X),

which induce equivalence on the hearts of the t-structures.

3.2.4.

Consider now the category
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)^{\heartsuit}
with its induced fiber functor. Denote the corresponding pro-algebraic group by

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev},0}(X).

Note, however, that since the abelian category
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)^{\heartsuit}
is semi-simple, the group
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev},0}(X)
is pro-reductive. Moreover,
since the entire category
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)
is semi-simple, the functor

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev},0}(X))\to\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)

is an equivalence.

3.2.5.

The functor

\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)\to\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)

induces a homomorphism:

(3.5)

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev}}(X)\to\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev},0}(X).

We claim:

Proposition 3.2.6
.

The map (
3.5
) factors through an isomorphism

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red,relev}}(X)\overset{\sim}{\to}\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev},0}(X).

In other words, Proposition
3.2.6
says that the map (
3.5
)
identifies
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev},0}(X)
with the maximal reductive quotient of

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev}}(X)
.

Proof.

The assertion of the proposition is equivalent to the following statement:

An object

V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev}}(X))^{\heartsuit}
is the restriction of an object in

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev,0}}(X))^{\heartsuit}
if and only if it is semi-simple.

In other words, an object
{\mathcal{F}}\in\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)^{\heartsuit}
belongs to

\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)^{\heartsuit}
if and only if it is semi-simple.

However, this is the content of Remark
2.7.6
.

∎

3.2.7.

According to Sect.
3.1.5
, the category
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
carries a canonical coaction
of the comonoidal category
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})
, compatible with the symmetric monoidal structure. Moreover, the coaction functor

\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\to\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})

is right t-exact (in fact, it is t-exact).

In particular,
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
carries a coaction of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})
with parallel properties.

3.2.8.

The key input from the previous two sections is that the above coaction of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})
on
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)

extends to a coaction
of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})
. This coaction has the following properties:

•

It is compatible with the coaction of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})
;

•

It is compatible with the symmetric monoidal structure
on
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
;

•

The coaction functor

\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\to\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\otimes{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})

is right t-exact (in fact, it is t-exact).

3.2.9.

In particular, we obtain a right t-exact (in fact, t-exact) symmetric monoidal endofunctor
[0]
of
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
,
which factors as

\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\overset{\psi}{\to}\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)\overset{\phi}{\to}\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X),

where
\phi
is the tautological forgetful functor.

The composition
\psi\circ\phi
is canonically isomorphic to the identity functor.

3.2.10.

Let us see what the above structure gives for the abelian category

(\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X))^{\heartsuit}\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev}}(X))^{\heartsuit}.

Since the fiber functor
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\to\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}
is compatible with the
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
-action,
we obtain that the algebraic group
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev}}(X)
acquires an action of
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
, which
contracts it to
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev},0}(X)
. This contraction gives rise to a
preferred
Levi
splitting

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev}}(X)\simeq\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev},0}(X)\ltimes\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev,unip}}(X).

Remark 3.2.11
.

The above action of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\subset{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
on
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev}}(X)
can also be viewed as follows.
The algebraic version of the Weil group is an extension

1\to\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev}}(X)\to\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg,loc.fin}}(X)\to{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}}\to 1,

so that

(\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X))^{\heartsuit}\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg,loc.fin}}(X))^{\heartsuit}.

The extension
\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg,loc.fin}}(X)
is induced from an extension

1\to\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev}}(X)\to\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}(X)\to{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}\to 1,

(\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X))^{\heartsuit}\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}(X))^{\heartsuit}.

The latter extension restricts to an extension

1\to\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev}}(X)\to\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,gr}}(X)\to{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to 1,

and we have

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,gr}}(X)\simeq(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev},0}(X)\times{\mathbb{G}}_{m})\ltimes\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev,unip}}(X).

Unwinding, we obtain

(\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev,gr}}(X))^{\heartsuit}\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,gr}}(X))^{\heartsuit}.

3.3.
The stack of local systems with restricted variation

In this subsection, we review the material from
[
AGKRRV
, Sects. 3.5-3.7]
.

3.3.1.

Let
{\mathsf{G}}
be a reductive group, and let
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
be as in
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 1.4]
.

By definition, for a
\mathsf{e}
-algebra
R
,

\operatorname{Maps}(\operatorname{Spec}(R),\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X))

is the groupoid of right t-exact symmetric monoidal functors

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathsf{G}})\to R{\operatorname{-mod}}(\operatorname{QLisse}(X)).

3.3.2.

Note that the
classical stack
underlying
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
identifies with the
classical stack
underlying

\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}),

where
\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(-,-)
is the stack of maps of pro-algebraic groups.

3.3.3.

The projection

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)\to\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red}}(X)

gives rise to a closed embedding
7
7

7

The first isomorphism in the formula below is due to the fact that the mapping
space between reductive groups is classical.

(3.6)

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X):=\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})\simeq\\
\simeq{}^{\operatorname{cl}}\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})\hookrightarrow{}^{\operatorname{cl}}\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})\simeq\\
\simeq{}^{\operatorname{cl}}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X).

At the level of
\mathsf{e}
-points, the image of (
3.6
) corresponds to
semi-simple
local systems, see
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 3.6]
.
Moreover, the map (
3.6
) induces a bijection between connected components.

3.3.4.

For future use we also note:

(1)

The automorphism group of a semi-simple local system is reductive;

(2)

For a connected component
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}
of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
, the intersection

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}:=\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\cap\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}

is the unique closed substack of
{}^{\operatorname{cl}}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}
that has a single isomorphism class of
\mathsf{e}
-points.

3.3.5.

Note that the map

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\to{}^{\operatorname{cl}}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)

admits a left inverse,

(3.7)

{}^{\operatorname{cl}}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\simeq{}^{\operatorname{cl}}\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})\to\\
\to{}^{\operatorname{cl}}\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})\simeq\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})=\\
=\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)

given by restriction along a choice of a Levi splitting

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)\simeq\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red}}(X)\ltimes\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{unip}}(X).

For a geometric point of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
, we will refer to its image under (
3.7
)
as its
semi-simplification
; it is well-defined up to isomorphism.

Thus, we have:

Lemma 3.3.6
.

Two geometric points of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
belong to the same connected component if and only if
they have isomorphic semi-simplifications.

3.3.7.

From now on we will take
k={\mathbb{F}}_{q}
. In this case, the action of
\operatorname{Frob}^{*}
on
\operatorname{QLisse}(X)
gives rise to an
automorphism of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
, which we denote by
\operatorname{Frob}
.

We set

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X):=(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}.

According to
[
AGKRRV
, Theorem 24.1.4]
, the prestack
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
is a disjoint union of
quasi-compact algebraic stacks, each of which can be written as a quotient of an affine scheme by an action of a reductive
group.

Remark 3.3.8
.

By definition, for an
\mathsf{e}
-algebra
R
,

\operatorname{Maps}(\operatorname{Spec}(R),\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X))

is the groupoid of right t-exact symmetric monoidal functors

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathsf{G}})\to R{\operatorname{-mod}}(\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)).

Note that in the above formula, it is really
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)
and not
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)
; the latter would produce a different object,
denoted
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm-restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
. For example, for
X=\operatorname{pt}
, we have:

•

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)=\operatorname{pt}/{\mathsf{G}}
;

•

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)={\mathsf{G}}/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})
;

•

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm-restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
is the union of formal neighborhoods of loci in
{\mathsf{G}}/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})
corresponding to elements in
{\mathsf{G}}

with a fixed semi-simple part.

3.4.
The substack of
relevant
local systems

3.4.1.

We now consider a variant of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
, denoted
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
, constructed in the
same way as
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
, with the difference that instead of the
gentle Tannakian

category
8
8

8

See
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 1.7]
for what this means.

\operatorname{QLisse}(X)
we use
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
.

As in Sect.
3.3.2
, the classical stack underlying
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
identifies with

{}^{\operatorname{cl}}\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}).

3.4.2.

The (fully faithful) embedding

\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{QLisse}(X)

gives rise to a
monomorphism

(3.8)

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X).

We claim:

Proposition 3.4.3
.

The map (
3.8
) is the inclusion of the union of some of the connected components.

Proof.

It is clear that the map (
3.8
) is formally étale
(i.e., it induces an isomorphism on cotangent spaces). Hence, it is enough to prove the corresponding assertion at the level of the
underlying classical substacks, i.e.,

{}^{\operatorname{cl}}\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})\to{}^{\operatorname{cl}}\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}).

For a connected classical scheme
S=\operatorname{Spec}(R)
, let us be given an
S
-family of homomorphisms

(3.9)

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)\overset{\phi_{S}}{\to}{\mathsf{G}}

such that for some
s\in S(\mathsf{e})
, the corresponding homomorphism
\phi_{s}
factors through

(3.10)

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)\twoheadrightarrow\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg,relev}}(X).

We need to show that (
3.9
) factors through (
3.10
).

The latter is equivalent to saying that for any
V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathsf{G}})^{\heartsuit}
, the corresponding object

\phi_{S}^{*}(V)\in R{\operatorname{-mod}}(\operatorname{QLisse}(X))^{\heartsuit}

belongs to
R{\operatorname{-mod}}(\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X))^{\heartsuit}
. For that, it is enough to
show that for every geometric point

\operatorname{Spec}(\mathsf{e}^{\prime})\overset{s^{\prime}}{\to}S,

the corresponding object
\phi_{s^{\prime}}^{*}(V)\in\mathsf{e}^{\prime}{\operatorname{-mod}}(\operatorname{QLisse}(X))^{\heartsuit}
belongs to

(3.11)

\mathsf{e}^{\prime}{\operatorname{-mod}}(\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X))\simeq\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}^{\prime}}\underset{\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}}{\otimes}\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X).

By Corollary
2.6.12
9
9

9

Note that here we are using an easy aspect of Corollary
2.6.12
, i.e., when
an object in question lies in the heart of the t-structure, i.e., we are not using the left-completeness property.
,
this is equivalent to the fact that all irreducible subquotients of all perverse cohomologies
of
\phi_{s^{\prime}}^{*}(V)
belong to (
3.11
). However, by Lemma
3.3.6
, these subquotients
are the same as the corresponding subquotients for
\phi_{s}(V)
(base changed
\mathsf{e}\rightsquigarrow\mathsf{e}^{\prime}
),
which belong to (
3.11
), by assumption.

∎

3.4.4.

Let
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
be a variant of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
constructed out of the gentle
Tannakian category
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)
. The symmetric monoidal functor

\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)\to\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)

gives rise to a map

(3.12)

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\to\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X).

We claim:

Proposition 3.4.5
.

The map (
3.12
) factors through an isomorphism

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\overset{\sim}{\to}\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\underset{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)}{\times}\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X).

Proof.

By Proposition
3.4.3
, the right-hand side is the union of connected components of

\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})

that correspond to homomorphisms that factor via

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red}}(X)\twoheadrightarrow\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red,relev}}(X).

The required assertion follows now from Proposition
3.2.6
.

∎

3.4.6.

Finally, we claim:

Lemma 3.4.7
.

The inclusion

(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}\hookrightarrow(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}=:\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

By Proposition
3.4.3
, a priori, the left-hand side is the union of some of the connected components
of the right hand side. Hence, in order to prove the lemma, we need to show that if an
\mathsf{e}
-point
\sigma
of

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
factors through

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
, then it belongs to
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
.

I.e., we need to show that for
V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathsf{G}})
, the corresponding object
V_{\sigma}\in\operatorname{QLisse}(X)

belongs to
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
. Note that by assumption,
V_{\sigma}
lies in the image
of

{\mathbf{oblv}}_{\operatorname{Weil}}:\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)\to\operatorname{QLisse}(X).

With no restriction of generality, we can assume that
V
is compact. Then
V_{\sigma}
is constructible.
Hence, we obtain that
V_{\sigma}
lies in the essential image of

\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)\to\operatorname{QLisse}(X).

Hence, it belongs to
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
.

∎

3.5.
The contraction

3.5.1.

Recall that according to Theorem
2.8.2
, the category
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
carries a coaction of
of the comonoidal category
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})
, and in particular of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})
.

By the functoriality of the construction in
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 1.8]
with respect to the gentle Tannakian category,
we obtain that the prestack
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)

carries an action of the pro-algebraic group
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
, which restricts to an action of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.

3.5.2.

In addition, the above coaction of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{G}}_{m})
on
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)

extends to a coaction of
{\operatorname{QCoh}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})
.

Applying again the functoriality of the construction in
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 1.8]
with respect to the gentle Tannakian category,
we obtain that
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
carries an action of the monoid
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
.

Moreover, this action is compatible
with the action of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
, and the resulting actions of

{\mathbb{A}}^{1}\hookleftarrow{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\hookrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}

coincide.

3.5.3.

Consider the resulting endomorphism
[0]
of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
. By construction, it factors as

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\overset{\psi}{\to}\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\overset{\phi}{\to}\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),

where:

•

\phi
is the map (
3.12
);

•

The map
\psi
is induced by the (right t-exact symmetric monoidal) functor

\psi:\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)\to\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X);

•

The maps
\phi
and
\psi
are
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
-equivariant, where the action on
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)
is via

{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}\twoheadrightarrow{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0}.

•

\psi\circ\phi\simeq\operatorname{id}
.

3.5.4.

In particular, we can view
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
as a prestack
over

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
by means
of
\psi
, and as such it carries a fiber-wise action of
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
.

We think of this structure as a
contraction
of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
to
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
.

3.6.
Contraction on the stack of arithmetic local systems

3.6.1.

Since the action of the monoid
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
on
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
commutes with the action of

{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
, and in particular of the element
\operatorname{Frob}
, it induces an action of
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
on the stack

(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}\overset{\text{Lemma~\ref{l:relev catches all}}}{\simeq}\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X).

3.6.2.

In particular, we obtain the endomorphism
[0]
of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
, which factors as

(3.13)

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\overset{\psi}{\to}(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}\overset{\phi}{\to}\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),

where we regard the stacks and maps in (
3.13
) as compatible with an action of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
, and where we identify

(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr,relev},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}\simeq(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}

thanks to Proposition
3.4.5
and Lemma
3.4.7
.

3.6.3.

As in Sect.
3.5.4
, we can thus view
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
as a prestack over
(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}
,
equipped with a fiber-wise action of
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
.

3.6.4.

Denote

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X):=(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}.

Note that by construction, for an
\mathsf{e}
-algebra
R
,

\operatorname{Maps}(\operatorname{Spec}(R),\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X))

is the groupoid of right t-exact symmetric monoidal functors

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathsf{G}})\to R{\operatorname{-mod}}((\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}).

Note also that the symmetric monoidal category
(\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}
appearing in the above formula
can be rewritten as

(3.14)

{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg},0})}{\otimes}\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X).

3.6.5.

Let us analyze the structure of the (prestack)
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
.

Let
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}
be a connected component of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
that intersects

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
nontrivially; this is equivalent to saying that
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}

is Frobenius-invariant.

Let
\sigma_{\alpha}
be the corresponding semi-simple local system, which is defined up to an isomorphism.
Denote
{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}:=\operatorname{Autom}(\sigma_{\alpha})
. By
Sect.
3.3.4
(1),
{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}
is reductive.

We can think about
\sigma_{\alpha}
as a homomorphism

\phi_{\alpha}:\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)\to{\mathsf{G}};

then
{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}:=Z_{\mathsf{G}}(\operatorname{Im}(\phi_{\alpha}))
.

The action of Frobenius on
\operatorname{QLisse}(X)
gives rise to an extension
10
10

10

Cf. Remark
3.2.11
.

(3.15)

1\to\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)\to\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)\to{\mathbb{Z}}\to 1.

Pick a lift of the element
1\in{\mathbb{Z}}
to
\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)
; denote it by
\operatorname{Frob}
.

By Sect.
3.3.4
(2), the fact that
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}
is Frobenius-invariant means
that so is the substack
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}
. I.e.,
\sigma_{\alpha}
admits a structure of Frobenius-equivariance.
This means that there exists an element
{\mathsf{g}}\in{\mathsf{G}}
such that the homomorphism
\phi_{\alpha}
intertwines
the action of
\operatorname{Frob}
on
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)
by conjugation and the adjoint action of
{\mathsf{g}}
.

The variety of such elements, to be denoted
{\mathsf{F}}_{\alpha}
, is a torsor with respect to
{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}
with respect to
both left and right multiplication. In particular, we have a well-defined
adjoint
action of
{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}
on
{\mathsf{F}}_{\alpha}
.

We identify

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}\simeq\operatorname{pt}/{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha},

and we obtain that

(3.16)

(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha})^{\operatorname{Frob}}\simeq{\mathsf{F}}_{\alpha}/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}).

3.6.6.

We now claim:

Proposition 3.6.7
.

The map
\psi
in (
3.13
) is affine.

Proof.

Let us base change the morphism in question by means of
\operatorname{pt}\to\operatorname{pt}/{\mathsf{G}}
, where we map both sides to
\operatorname{pt}/{\mathsf{G}}

by composing the forgetful map to
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
with the map

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\to\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{restr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(\operatorname{pt})\simeq\operatorname{pt}/{\mathsf{G}},

corresponding to a point
x\in X
.

It suffices to show that for every connected component
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}
of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
,

the resulting map

(3.17)

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}\underset{\operatorname{pt}/{\mathsf{G}}}{\times}\operatorname{pt}\to\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}\underset{\operatorname{pt}/{\mathsf{G}}}{\times}\operatorname{pt}

is affine. We claim that both prestacks appearing in (
3.17
) are in fact affine schemes.

Indeed,

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}\underset{\operatorname{pt}/{\mathsf{G}}}{\times}\operatorname{pt}

is an affine scheme by
[
AGKRRV
, Theorem 24.1.4]
.

The fiber product
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}\underset{\operatorname{pt}/{\mathsf{G}}}{\times}\operatorname{pt}
is an affine scheme by (
3.16
):
in fact, it is isomorphic to

({\mathsf{F}}_{\alpha}\times{\mathsf{G}})/{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha},

which is affine since
{\mathsf{G}}/{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}
is affine (being the quotient of a reductive group by a reductive subgroup).

∎

3.7.
The excursion algebra

3.7.1.

We are now ready to state and prove the main result of this paper:

Theorem 3.7.2
.

The map

(3.18)

\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)})\to\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)})

is an isomorphism.

Note that by combining Theorem
3.7.2
and (
3.16
) we obtain:

Corollary 3.7.3
.

For every connected component
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}
of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
, the
algebra

\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})_{\alpha}:=\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha},{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}})

is classical, integral and normal.

Proof.

Indeed, the algebras

\Gamma({\mathsf{F}}_{\alpha}/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}),{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathsf{F}}_{\alpha}/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha})})\simeq\Gamma({\mathsf{F}}_{\alpha},{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathsf{F}}_{\alpha}})^{{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}}

have this property, since
since
{\mathsf{F}}_{\alpha}
is a torsor over
{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}
, while
{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}
is reductive.

∎

3.7.4.

For a connected component
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}
of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
, the algebra

\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})_{\alpha}

appearing in Corollary
3.7.3
is called the

Excursion Algebra
(corresponding to the given connected component).

Denote

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}:=\operatorname{Spec}(\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})_{\alpha}).

Thus, Corollary
3.7.3
says that each connected component of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}
is classical, integral and normal.

3.7.5.

We now begin the proof of Theorem
3.7.2
. The first step of the proof is an assertion of independent interest.

Since
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}
is a quotient of an affine scheme by an algebraic group, the functor

\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha},-)

preserves colimits. Hence, the
action of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
on
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}
allows us to upgrade
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})_{\alpha}
to
an object of
