{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})
.

Note, however, that the action of
\operatorname{Frob}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
on
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}
is tautologically trivial. Hence, the image
of
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})_{\alpha}
under the functor

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})\to{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}

is multiple of the trivial representation.

However, since the above functor is fully faithful, we obtain:

Lemma 3.7.6
.

The object
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})_{\alpha}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}})
is a multiple of the trivial representation.

Corollary 3.7.7
.

The action of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
on
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})_{\alpha}
obtained by restriction along
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\to{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}

is trivial.

3.7.8.

From Corollary
3.7.7
we obtain that in order to prove Theorem
3.7.2
, it suffices to show that the map

(3.19)

\left(\Gamma\left(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)}\right)\right)^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\to\left(\Gamma\left(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)}\right)\right)^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}},

induced by (
3.18
), is an isomorphism.

3.7.9.

Recall the map

\psi:\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\to\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X).

It suffices to show that the map

(3.20)

\left(\Gamma\left(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)}\right)\right)^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\to\left(\Gamma\left(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)}\right)\right)^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}},

given by pullback along
\psi
, is an isomorphism.

3.7.10.

We rewrite

\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)})\simeq\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),\psi_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)})).

The map (
3.20
) is obtained by applying
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-invariants to the map

\Gamma\left(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)}\right)\to\Gamma\left(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),\psi_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)})\right).

3.7.11.

Recall now (see Sect.
3.6.3
) that we can view the action of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
on
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)

as
fiberwise
for the projection
\psi
. Moreover, this action is
contracting
, i.e., extends to a fiber-wise action of

{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
, and the action of
0\in{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
is the composition
\phi\circ\psi
.

The map (
3.20
) is obtained by applying
\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),-)

to the map

(3.21)

{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)}\simeq({\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)})^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}\to\psi_{*}({\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)})^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}.

It suffices to show that the map (
3.21
) is an isomorphism. However, this follows from
Proposition
3.6.7
: indeed we are dealing with an affine map with a fiberwise contracting action of
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
.

∎[Theorem
3.7.2
]

Remark 3.7.12
.

Here is a different argument proving that (
3.19
) is an isomorphism (one that avoids Proposition
3.6.7
):

Note that the composition
(
3.19
)
\circ
(
3.20
) is the identity map. So it is enough to show that
the composition (
3.20
)
\circ
(
3.19
) is the identity map.

Now, the composition

(3.22)

\Gamma\left(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)}\right)\overset{\phi^{*}}{\to}\\
\to\Gamma\left(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)}\right)\overset{\psi^{*}}{\to}\Gamma\left(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)}\right)

equals
[0]^{*}
.

We can regard
\Gamma\left(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)}\right)

as a representation of the monoid
{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
, so that the map
[0]^{*}
above is the action of point
0\in{\mathbb{A}}^{1}
.

Now, for any
W\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{A}}^{1})
, the action of
0
on
W^{{\mathbb{G}}_{m}}
is the identity, while
the composition (
3.20
)
\circ
(
3.19
) is obtained from (
3.22
)
by taking
{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
-invariants.

4.
Properties of the excursion algebra

In this section we will connect our definition of the excursion algebra to (a variant of) V. Lafforgue’s definition.

In addition, we will study its finiteness properties over the
Hecke
algebra.

4.1.
A change of conventions

4.1.1.

The contents of the previous section admit the following variant:

We replace the category
\operatorname{QLisse}(X)
by its variant, where we bound the degree of ramification
(at the generic point of each boundary divisor for a given compactification of
X
).

4.1.2.

That said, we will
not
change the notation, and denote the resulting category
by the same symbol
\operatorname{QLisse}(X)
.

Correspondingly, all the variants (e.g.,
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev}}(X)
,
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)
), as well as the
moduli spaces that arise from them (e.g.,
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{retsr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
,
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
),
should be understood in this new sense.

4.1.3.

The impact of the restriction on ramification is the following: the stack
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)

has now
finitely many
connected components, see
[
EK
]
.

4.1.4.

Denote

\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}}):=\underset{\alpha}{\Pi}\,\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})_{\alpha};

this is the “total” excursion algebra, whose properties we will study
in this section.

Denote also

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X):=\operatorname{Spec}(\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}}))\simeq\underset{\alpha}{\sqcup}\,\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}.

4.2.
Relation to V. Lafforgue’s definition

4.2.1.

Let
\operatorname{Weil}(X)
denote the Weil group of
X
:

\operatorname{Weil}(X):=\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)\underset{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}}{\times}{\mathbb{Z}},

equipped with its natural topology.

Let
\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X)
(resp.,
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X)
) be the group
\operatorname{Weil}(X)
(resp.,
\operatorname{Gal}(X)
), viewed as a discrete group.
Recall the group
\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)
, see (
3.15
). By construction, we have

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X))^{\heartsuit}\simeq\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)^{\heartsuit}.

We have tautological homomorphisms

(4.1)

\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X)\to\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)\text{ and }\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X)\to\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X).

The first of the above homomorphisms gives rise to a functor

(4.2)

\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil}}(X)^{\heartsuit}\to{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X))^{\heartsuit}.

The functor (
4.2
) is fully faithful, and its essential image
consists of representations, on which the action of the subgroup

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X)\subset\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X)

is locally finite and as such corresponds to a continuous action of
\operatorname{Gal}(X)
;
in other words, the resulting object of
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X))^{\heartsuit}
corresponds to
an object of
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X))^{\heartsuit}
.

4.2.2.

Let
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,discr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
be the stack

\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}).

The functor (
4.2
) gives rise to a map

(4.3)

^{\operatorname{cl}}\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)\to{}^{\operatorname{cl}}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,discr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X).

The following is straightforward:

Lemma 4.2.3
.

(a)
The map (
4.3
) is a closed embedding.

(b) An
\mathsf{e}
-point of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,discr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
is the image of an
\mathsf{e}
-point of
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)

if and only if the corresponding homomorphism
\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X)\to{\mathsf{G}}
extends to a homomorphism

\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)\to{\mathsf{G}}
.

4.2.4.

Denote

\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{\operatorname{discr}}:=\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,discr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,discr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)}).

Note that
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,discr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
is the quotient of an affine scheme (of infinite type)
by the action of
{\mathsf{G}}
. In particular,
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{\operatorname{discr}}
is connective.

Denote

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,discr,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X):=\operatorname{Spec}(\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{\operatorname{discr}}).

4.2.5.

The map (
4.3
) gives rise to a map

(4.4)

H^{0}(\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{\operatorname{discr}})\to H^{0}(\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})).

Note, however, that since
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{\operatorname{discr}}
is connective and

\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
is classical, the datum of a map as in (
4.4
) is
equivalent to that of a map

\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{\operatorname{discr}}\to\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}}).

Equivalently, we obtain a map

(4.5)

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\to\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,discr,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X).

From Lemma
4.2.3
(a), we obtain:

Corollary 4.2.6
.

The map (
4.5
) is a closed embedding.

Proof.

Follows from the fact that the map in (
4.3
) arises from a map
between affine schemes, by passing to stack-theoretic quotients by the reductive group
{\mathsf{G}}
.

∎

4.2.7.

Recall now (see
[
VLaf
, Lemma 11.9]
) that the tautological projection

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,discr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\to\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,discr,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)

gives rise to a bijection between:

•

Isomorphism classes of
semi-simple
homomorphisms
\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X)\to{\mathsf{G}}
, and

•

\mathsf{e}
-points of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,discr,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
.

Similarly, the projection

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\to\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)

gives rise to a bijection between:

•

Isomorphism classes of
semi-simple
homomorphisms
\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)\to{\mathsf{G}}
, and

•

\mathsf{e}
-points of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
.

4.2.8.

Let

{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\subset\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,discr,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)

be the Zariski closure of the subset of
\mathsf{e}
-points that correspond to
continuous

semi-simple homomorphisms
\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)\to{\mathsf{G}}
.

We claim:

Proposition 4.2.9
.

The map (
4.5
) factors via an isomorphism

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\overset{\sim}{\to}{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X).

Proof.

First we show that the map (
4.5
) factors via
{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,discr}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
.
Since
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
is reduced, it is enough to show that the image
of the set of
\mathsf{e}
-points of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
under (
4.5
)
is contained in the set of
\mathsf{e}
-points of
{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
. However,
this follows from the description of the former set in Sect.
4.2.7
and
Lemma
4.2.3
(b).

In order to show that the resulting map

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\to{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)

is an isomorphism, it is enough to show that it is surjective at the level of
\mathsf{e}
-points.
However, this again follows from Sect.
4.2.7
and
Lemma
4.2.3
(b).

∎

4.3.
Excursion vs Hecke operators

4.3.1.

Fix a representation
{\mathsf{G}}\to GL_{n}
, i.e., an object
V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathsf{G}})^{\heartsuit,c}
.

To a conjugacy class

\gamma\in\operatorname{Weil}(X)
we attach an element

\operatorname{exc}(V,\gamma)^{\operatorname{discr}}\in H^{0}(\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{\operatorname{discr}})

whose value on
\sigma\in\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})
equals
\operatorname{Tr}(\sigma(\gamma),V)
.

Let

\operatorname{exc}(V,\gamma)\in\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})\simeq\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)})

denote the image of
\operatorname{exc}(V,\gamma)^{\operatorname{discr}}
under (
4.4
).

4.3.2.

Let
x^{\prime}
be a closed point of
X
, and let
\operatorname{Frob}_{x^{\prime}}\in\operatorname{Weil}(X)
be the conjugacy class
of the corresponding Frobenius element. We will use the notation

\operatorname{H}(V,x^{\prime})^{\operatorname{discr}}:=\operatorname{exc}(V,\operatorname{Frob}_{x^{\prime}})^{\operatorname{discr}}\text{ and }\operatorname{H}(V,x^{\prime}):=\operatorname{exc}(V,\operatorname{Frob}_{x^{\prime}}).

The elements

\operatorname{H}(V,x^{\prime})\in\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})

are called the
Hecke operators
.

4.3.3.

The assignment

V\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{H}(V,x^{\prime})

defines a homomorphism

(4.6)

{\mathcal{H}}_{x^{\prime}}({\mathsf{G}})\to\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}}),

where

{\mathcal{H}}_{x^{\prime}}({\mathsf{G}}):=\mathsf{e}\underset{{\mathbb{Z}}}{\otimes}K^{0}({\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathsf{G}})^{\heartsuit,c})\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathsf{G}}/\!/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})}.

The homomorphisms (
4.6
) combine to a homomorphism

(4.7)

{\mathcal{H}}_{X}({\mathsf{G}}):=\underset{x^{\prime}\in|X|}{\bigotimes}\,{\mathcal{H}}_{x^{\prime}}({\mathsf{G}})\to\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}}).

4.3.4.

Assume for a moment that
{\mathsf{G}}=GL_{n}
and that
V
is the tautological representation. In this case
we denote

\operatorname{exc}(V,\gamma)^{\operatorname{discr}}=:\operatorname{exc}(\gamma)^{\operatorname{discr}}_{\operatorname{taut}}\text{ and }\operatorname{exc}(V,\gamma)=:\operatorname{exc}(\gamma)_{\operatorname{taut}}.

The following is well-known (see, e.g.,
[
Pro
]
):

Lemma 4.3.5
.

The elements
\operatorname{exc}(\gamma)^{\operatorname{discr}}_{\operatorname{taut}}
,
\gamma\in\operatorname{Weil}(X)
generate
H^{0}(\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{\operatorname{discr}})
.

Corollary 4.3.6
.

The elements
\operatorname{exc}(\gamma)_{\operatorname{taut}}
,
\gamma\in\operatorname{Weil}(X)
generate
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
.

4.3.7.

Let
{\mathsf{G}}
again be arbitrary. Let us be given a data of
(I,V_{I},\gamma_{I},v_{I},\xi_{I})
, where:

•

I
is a finite set;

•

V_{I}
is a finite-dimensional representation of
{\mathsf{G}}^{I}
;

•

\gamma_{I}
is an
I
-tuple of elements of
\operatorname{Weil}(X)
(defined up to simultaneous conjugation);

•

v_{I}
is a vector in
V^{I}
, invariant with respect to the diagonal copy of
{\mathsf{G}}
;

•

\xi_{I}
is a covector on
V^{I}
, invariant with respect to the diagonal copy of
{\mathsf{G}}
.

To this data we assign an element

\operatorname{exc}(V_{I},\gamma_{I},v_{I},\xi_{I})^{\operatorname{discr}}\in H^{0}(\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{\operatorname{discr}})

as follows:

The value of
\operatorname{exc}(V_{I},\gamma_{I},v_{I},\xi_{I})^{\operatorname{discr}}
on
\sigma\in\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})

the scalar
\xi(\gamma_{I}(v_{I}))
.

Let
\operatorname{exc}(V_{I},\gamma_{I},v_{I},\xi_{I})
denote the image of
\operatorname{exc}(V_{I},\gamma_{I},v_{I},\xi_{I})^{\operatorname{discr}}
under the homomorphism
(
4.4
).

The elements
\operatorname{exc}(V_{I},\gamma_{I},v_{I},\xi_{I})
are called the
excursion operators
.

Remark 4.3.8
.

Note that the elements
\operatorname{exc}(V,\gamma)^{\operatorname{discr}}
(resp.,
\operatorname{exc}(V,\gamma)
) are a particular case of

\operatorname{exc}(V_{I},\gamma_{I},v_{I},\xi_{I})^{\operatorname{discr}}
(resp.,
\operatorname{exc}(V_{I},\gamma_{I},v_{I},\xi_{I})
), where:

•

I=\{1,2\}
;

•

V_{I}=V\otimes V^{\vee}
;

•

\gamma_{I}=(\gamma,1)
;

•

v_{I}
is the unit vector in
V\otimes V^{\vee}
;

•

\xi_{I}
is the counit covector.

4.3.9.

The following is established in
[
VLaf
, Sect. 11]
(see also
[
GKRV
, Sect. 2]
):

Proposition 4.3.10
.

The elements
\operatorname{exc}(V_{I},\gamma_{I},v_{I},\xi_{I})^{\operatorname{discr}}
span
H^{0}(\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{\operatorname{discr}})
.

Corollary 4.3.11
.

The elements
\operatorname{exc}(V_{I},\gamma_{I},v_{I},\xi_{I})
span
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
.

4.4.
Finiteness and generation properties

4.4.1.

The goal of the rest of this section is to prove the following:

Theorem 4.4.2
.

(a) For any
x^{\prime}\in X
, the homomorphism

{\mathcal{H}}_{x^{\prime}}({\mathsf{G}})\overset{\text{\eqref{e:Hecke homomorphism}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})

makes
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
into a
finitely generated

{\mathcal{H}}_{x^{\prime}}({\mathsf{G}})
-module.

(b) Let
{\mathsf{G}}={\mathop{\operatorname{\rm GL}}}_{n}
. Then the homomorphism

{\mathcal{H}}_{X}({\mathsf{G}})\overset{\text{\eqref{e:Hecke Homomorphism}}}{\longrightarrow}\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})

is surjective.

4.4.3.

Let us first show how point (a) of Theorem
4.4.2
implies point (b). Indeed, given point (a), it suffices to prove the following:

Given a homomorphism
{\mathcal{H}}_{X}({\mathsf{G}})\overset{\underline{\lambda}}{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{e}
, the map

\mathsf{e}\to\mathsf{e}\underset{{\mathcal{H}}_{X}({\mathsf{G}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})

is surjective.

By Corollary
4.3.6
, it suffices to show that for every
\gamma\in\operatorname{Weil}(X)
, the image of

\operatorname{exc}(\gamma)_{\operatorname{taut}}
in

(4.8)

\mathsf{e}\underset{{\mathcal{H}}_{X}({\mathsf{G}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})

is constant.

4.4.4.

Since
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
is the quotient of an affine scheme by an action of a reductive group, the algebra
(
4.8
) is the algebra of global functions on the fiber product

(4.9)

\operatorname{pt}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathcal{H}}_{X}({\mathsf{G}}))}{\times}\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X).

Hence, it suffices to show that for every
\gamma\in\operatorname{Weil}(X)
, the function on
(
4.9
) obtained by restriction from

\operatorname{exc}(\gamma)_{\operatorname{taut}}\in\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})\simeq\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)})

is constant.

4.4.5.

Since
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
is an algebraic stack locally of finite type, it suffices to establish the following two properties
for any
\gamma\in\operatorname{Weil}(X)
:

(i) For a pair of
\mathsf{e}
-points
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
of (
4.9
), the values of

\operatorname{exc}(\gamma)_{\operatorname{taut}}
at these points are equal;

(ii) For a local Artinian
\mathsf{e}
-algebra
R
and an
R
-point
\sigma_{R}
of (
4.9
),
the pullback of
\operatorname{exc}(\gamma)_{\operatorname{taut}}
along
\sigma_{R}
belongs to
\mathsf{e}\subset R
.

4.4.6.

We can think of
\sigma_{i}
as a homomorphism

\phi_{i}:\operatorname{Weil}(X)\to GL_{n}(\mathsf{e}),

whose restriction to
\operatorname{Gal}(X)
is
continuous
.

Similarly, we can think of
\sigma_{R}
as a homomorphism

\phi_{R}:\operatorname{Weil}(X)\to GL_{n}(R),

whose restriction to
\operatorname{Gal}(X)
is
continuous
(for the natural topology on
GL_{n}(R)
).

The value of
\operatorname{exc}(\gamma)_{\operatorname{taut}}
on
\sigma_{i}
is
\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{i}(\gamma))
, and the pullback of

\operatorname{exc}(\gamma)_{\operatorname{taut}}
along
\sigma_{R}
is
\operatorname{Tr}_{R}(\phi_{R}(\gamma))
. Thus, we need to show:

(i’)
\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{1}(\gamma))=\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{2}(\gamma))
, and

(ii’)
\operatorname{Tr}_{R}(\phi_{R}(\gamma))\in\mathsf{e}\subset R
.

4.4.7.

As as a warm-up we will now prove assertions (i’) and (ii’) in the special case when the above homomorphisms
extend to
continuous
homomorphisms from
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)
.

By Chebotarev’s density, we can find a sequence of points
\gamma^{\prime}_{\alpha}
that converge to
\gamma
in the topology
of
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)
, such that the conjugacy class of each
\gamma^{\prime}_{\alpha}
is of the form
\operatorname{Frob}_{x^{\prime}_{\alpha}}

for some
x^{\prime}_{\alpha}\in|X|
.

By assumption, the values of
\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{i}(\gamma))\in\mathsf{e}
(
i=1,2
) is the limit of
\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{i}(\gamma^{\prime}_{\alpha}))
in the topology of
\mathsf{e}
.
Similarly, the value of
\operatorname{Tr}_{R}(\phi_{R}(\gamma))\in R
is the limit of
\operatorname{Tr}_{R}(\phi_{R}(\gamma^{\prime}_{\alpha}))

in the topology of
R
.

The fact that
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
both map to (
4.9
) implies that

\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{1}(\gamma^{\prime}_{\alpha}))=\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{2}(\gamma^{\prime}_{\alpha})),\quad\forall\alpha.

Hence,

\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{1}(\gamma))=\operatorname{Tr}(\phi_{2}(\gamma)),

as required.

The above establishes property (i’). For property (ii’) we note that

\operatorname{Tr}_{R}(\phi_{R}(\gamma^{\prime}_{\alpha}))\in\mathsf{e}\subset R

and hence the same is true for
\operatorname{Tr}_{R}(\phi_{R}(\gamma))
.

4.5.
Proof of Property (i’)

The argument below was explained to us by V. Lafforgue. It is an upgrade to Weil sheaves of the well-known argument
(see
[
Laum
, Theorem 1.1.2]
) that the map from the Grothendieck group of étale sheaves to
\underset{n}{\Pi}\,\mathsf{Funct}(X({\mathbb{F}}_{q^{n}}),\mathsf{e})

is injective.

4.5.1.

We will think of
\phi_{i}
as an
n
-dimensional representation
V_{i}
of
\operatorname{Weil}(X)
; let

\underset{a}{\oplus}\,V_{i,a}

be the direct sum of its irreducible subquotients.

For each
a
we can find an element
\mu_{i,a}\in\mathsf{e}^{\times}
, such that the representation

V_{i,a}\otimes\chi_{\mu_{i,a}},

extends to a continuous representation of
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)
, where
\chi_{\mu_{i,a}}
is the
character of
\operatorname{Weil}(X)
that factors as

\operatorname{Weil}(X)\to{\mathbb{Z}}\overset{1\mapsto\mu_{i,a}}{\longrightarrow}\mathsf{e}^{\times}.

For each value
r
of the norm, set

V_{i,r}:=\underset{a,\,|\mu_{i,a}|=r}{\oplus}\,V_{i,a}.

It suffices to show that for every
r
,

\operatorname{Tr}(\gamma,V_{1,r})=\operatorname{Tr}(\gamma,V_{2,r}).

4.5.2.

Choose some
\mu_{r}
with
|\mu_{r}|=r
, and set

V^{c}_{i,r}:=V_{i,r}\otimes\chi_{\mu_{r}}.

It suffices to show that for every
r
,

(4.10)

\operatorname{Tr}(\gamma,V^{c}_{1,r})=\operatorname{Tr}(\gamma,V^{c}_{2,r}).

Note, however, that each
V^{c}_{i,r}
extends to a continuous representation of
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)
.
Hence, by the argument in Sect.
4.4.7
, in order to prove (
4.10
), it suffices to show that

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}_{x^{\prime}},V^{c}_{1,r})=\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}_{x^{\prime}},V^{c}_{2,r}),\quad\forall\,x^{\prime}\in|X|.

The latter is equivalent to

(4.11)

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}_{x^{\prime}},V_{1,r})=\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}_{x^{\prime}},V_{2,r}),\quad\forall\,x^{\prime}\in|X|.

4.5.3.

For
x^{\prime}\in|X|
, let
\lambda_{i,a,x^{\prime},k}
,
k=1,...,\dim(V_{i,a})
denote the eigenvalues of
\operatorname{Frob}_{x^{\prime}}
on
V_{i,a}
.
Denote

\lambda^{c}_{i,a,x^{\prime},k}:=\lambda_{i,a,x^{\prime},k}\cdot\mu^{\deg(x^{\prime})}_{i,a}.

The fact that
\sigma_{1}
and
\sigma_{2}
both map to (
4.9
) implies that

(4.12)

\underset{a}{\Sigma}\,\underset{k}{\Sigma}\,(\lambda^{c}_{1,a,x^{\prime},k})^{m}\cdot\mu_{1,a}^{-m\cdot\deg(x^{\prime})}=\underset{a}{\Sigma}\,\underset{k}{\Sigma}\,(\lambda^{c}_{2,a,x^{\prime},k})^{m}\cdot\mu_{2,a}^{-m\cdot\deg(x^{\prime})},\quad\forall m\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\geq 1}.

This implies that the sets

\{\lambda^{c}_{1,a,x^{\prime},k}\cdot\mu^{-\deg(x^{\prime})}_{1,a}\}\text{ and }\{\lambda^{c}_{2,a,x^{\prime},k}\cdot\mu^{-\deg(x^{\prime})}_{2,a}\},

counted with multiplicities, agree.

Note that the elements
\lambda^{c}_{i,a,x^{\prime},k}
have norm
1
in
\mathsf{e}
. Hence, we obtain
that for each value
r
of the norm, the sets

\{\lambda^{c}_{1,a,x^{\prime},k}\cdot\mu^{-\deg(x^{\prime})}_{1,a}\,|\,|\mu_{1,a}|=r\}\text{ and }\{\lambda^{c}_{2,a,x^{\prime},k}\cdot\mu^{-\deg(x^{\prime})}_{2,a}\,|\,|\mu_{2,a}|=r\}

also agree. And hence

\underset{a,\,|\mu_{i,a}|=r}{\Sigma}\,\underset{k}{\Sigma}\,(\lambda^{c}_{1,a,x^{\prime},k})^{m}\cdot\mu_{1,a}^{-m\cdot\deg(x^{\prime})}=\underset{a,\,|\mu_{i,a}|=r}{\Sigma}\,\underset{k}{\Sigma}\,(\lambda^{c}_{2,a,x^{\prime},k})^{m}\cdot\mu_{2,a}^{-m\cdot\deg(x^{\prime})},\quad\forall m\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\geq 1}.

The latter equality means that for every
r
,

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}_{x^{\prime}}^{m},V_{1,r})=\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}_{x^{\prime}}^{m},V_{2,r}),\quad\forall m\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{\geq 1}.

Taking
m=1
, the latter equality gives the desired equality (
4.11
).

∎[Property (i’)]

4.6.
Proof of Property (ii)

4.6.1.

Let us regard
\phi_{R}
as a representation
V_{R}
of
\operatorname{Weil}(X)
over the ring
R
; let
V
be its reduction modulo
m_{R}
,
the maximal ideal of
R
.

By the argument in
[
AGKRRV
, Sect. 25.3]
, we can equip
V
with a filtration

0=V_{0}\subset V_{1}\subset....\subset V_{k-1}\subset V_{k}=V,

such that the deformation

V\rightsquigarrow V_{R}

extends to a filtered deformation

V_{i}\rightsquigarrow V_{i,R}.

This allows us to replace
V
by

\operatorname{gr}(V):=\underset{i}{\oplus}\,V_{i}/V_{i-1}=:\underset{i}{\oplus}\,V^{\prime}_{i},

and
V_{R}
by

\underset{i}{\oplus}\,V_{i,R}/V_{i-1,R}=:\underset{i}{\oplus}\,V^{\prime}_{i,R}.

Denote the corresponding
R
-point of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
by
\sigma^{\prime}_{R}
. This point factors through
(
4.9
), and we can replace the original
\sigma_{R}
by
\sigma^{\prime}_{R}
.

4.6.2.

Let
\mu_{i}
be as in Sect.
4.5.1
, i.e.,

V^{\prime}_{i}\otimes\chi_{\mu_{i}}

extends to a continuous representation of
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)
.

For every value
r
of the norm, set

V^{\prime}_{r,R}:=\underset{i,\,|\mu_{i}|=r}{\oplus}\,V^{\prime}_{i,R}.

Denote

n_{r}:=\underset{i,\,|\mu_{i}|=r}{\Sigma}\,\dim(V^{\prime}_{i}).

We obtain that
\sigma^{\prime}_{R}
factors through an
R
-point of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{{\mathsf{M}}}(X)
, where
{\mathsf{M}}
is the Levi subgroup of
{\mathsf{G}}=GL_{n}

{\mathsf{M}}:=\underset{r}{\Pi}\,GL(n_{r}).

Note now that the map

\operatorname{Spec}({\mathcal{H}}_{X}({\mathsf{M}}))\to\operatorname{Spec}({\mathcal{H}}_{X}({\mathsf{G}}))

is formally étale at the image of the point

\sigma^{\prime}:=\sigma^{\prime}_{R}|_{\operatorname{pt}}

under
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{{\mathsf{M}}}(X)\to\operatorname{Spec}({\mathcal{H}}_{X}({\mathsf{M}}))
.

This implies that
\sigma^{\prime}_{R}
lifts to a point of

\operatorname{pt}\underset{\operatorname{Spec}({\mathcal{H}}_{X}({\mathsf{M}}))}{\times}\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{M}}(X).

This allows us to reduce the proof to the case when only one value of
r
appears.

4.6.3.

Let
\mu_{r}
be such that
|\mu_{r}|=r
. For
i
with
|\mu_{i}|=r
,
denote

V^{\prime}_{i}{}^{c}:=V^{\prime}_{i}\otimes\chi_{\mu_{r}}.

Note that
V^{\prime}_{i}{}^{c}
also extends to a continuous representation of
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)
.
Hence, so does

V^{\prime}_{r,R}\otimes\chi_{\mu_{r}}.

Thus, translating by
\mu_{r}
on
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
and
\operatorname{Spec}({\mathcal{H}}_{X}({\mathsf{G}}))
, we can assume that

V^{\prime}_{r,R}
is continuous. This reduces us to the case considered in Sect.
4.4.7
.

∎[Property (i”)]

4.7.
Proof of Theorem
4.4.2
(a)

4.7.1.

Since
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
has finitely many connected components, it suffices to show that for every connected
component
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)_{\alpha}
, the algebra
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})_{\alpha}
is finite as a module over
{\mathcal{H}}_{x^{\prime}}({\mathsf{G}})
.

By Theorem
3.7.2
and (
3.16
), it suffices to show that the map

{\mathsf{F}}_{\alpha}/\!/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha})\to{\mathsf{G}}/\!/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})

is finite.

4.7.2.

Choose a point
{\mathsf{g}}\in{\mathsf{F}}_{\alpha}
. This point normalizes
{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}
, and we can identify

{\mathsf{F}}_{\alpha}\simeq{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha},

so that the adjoint action of
{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}
on
{\mathsf{F}}_{\alpha}
corresponds to the
\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{g}})
-twisted
conjugation action of
{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}
on itself.

This, we have to show that the map

(4.13)

{\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha}/\operatorname{Ad}_{\mathsf{g}}({\mathsf{G}}_{\alpha})\to{\mathsf{G}}/\!/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})

is finite.

In fact, we will prove a more general group-theoretic statement, which we will now formulate.

4.7.3.

Let
\phi
be an automorphism of a reductive group
G
which preserves a maximal torus
T\subseteq G
. Then we obtain an action of
\phi
on the weight lattice
X^{*}(T)
of
T
. Suppose that
\phi
acts on
X^{*}(T)
with finite order. Then we claim that for any
n\in N(T)
, the automorphism
\operatorname{Ad}_{n}\circ\phi
acts on
X^{*}(T)
with finite order as well. This follows because if
\phi
preserves
T
, then it automatically preserves
N(T)
, so the action of
W
on
X^{*}(T)
extends to an action of

W\rtimes\left\langle\phi\right\rangle

on
X^{*}(T)
, and since both
W
and
\left\langle\phi\right\rangle
are finite, any element of this group acts on
X^{*}(T)
with finite order.

Thus, if
\phi
is an automorphism of a reductive group
G
, we will say that
\phi
acts with finite order on
X^{*}(T)
if for some/any
g\in G
such that
\operatorname{Ad}_{g}\circ\phi
preserves
T
, the resulting automorphism of
X^{*}(T)
has finite order.

Proposition 4.7.4
.

Let
\phi
be an automorphism of a reductive group
G
such that
\phi
acts with finite order on
X^{*}(T)
.
Then if
H
is any reductive subgroup of
G
preserved by
\phi
, the map

H/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(H)\to G/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(G)

is finite.

4.7.5.

Clearly, Proposition
4.7.4
contains the assertion that (
4.13
) is finite as a particular case.

∎[Theorem
4.4.2
(a)]

4.8.
Proof of Proposition
4.7.4

4.8.1.

Note that when
\phi
is the identity automorphism of
G
, Proposition
4.7.4
recovers
[
Vin
, Theorem 1]
in the case when
p=1
.

After some preliminary remarks, we will prove Proposition
4.7.4
in five steps, starting with the assumption that
G
is connected and
H=T
is a maximal torus of
G
, and then gradually relaxing these assumptions.

4.8.2.

Suppose that
G
is a connected reductive group with an automorphism
\phi
. Recall the Peter-Weyl theorem

{\mathcal{O}}_{G}\simeq\bigoplus_{\sigma}V_{\sigma}\otimes V_{\sigma}^{\vee}\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G\times G).

If
G
acts on itself by
\phi
-twisted conjugacy, then we obtain instead

{\mathcal{O}}_{G}\simeq\bigoplus_{\sigma}V_{\sigma}\otimes V_{\phi(\sigma)}^{\vee}.

It follows from Schur’s lemma that

{\mathcal{O}}_{G/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(G)}\simeq\bigoplus_{\sigma=\phi(\sigma)}k

where for each
\sigma
that is isomorphic to its image under
\phi
, corresponding copy of
k
is spanned by the function

g\mapsto\operatorname{tr}_{V}(\alpha\cdot g)

for any
\phi
-equivariance data
\alpha
for
V
.

4.8.3.

Suppose that
T
is a subtorus of
G
which is preserved by
\phi
. Applying the discussion of the previous paragraph, we have

(4.14)

{\mathcal{O}}_{T/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(T)}\simeq\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}\lambda\in X^{*}(T)\\
\lambda=\phi(\lambda)\end{subarray}}k

If
V
is any irreducible representation of
G
with
V\simeq V^{\phi}
, we observe that the function

t\mapsto\operatorname{tr}_{V}(\alpha\cdot t)

only receives contributions from the weights in
V
which are stable under the action of
\phi
.

We now begin the proof of Proposition
4.7.4
.

4.8.4.
Step 1

G
is connected and
H=T
is a maximal torus of
G
. We want to show that the map
T/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(T)\to G/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(G)
is finite. Because it is affine, it suffices to show that it is proper. We will prove this using the valuative criterion. Suppose that
R
is a valuation ring with fraction field
K
, and suppose that we have a commutative diagram:

(4.15)

\begin{CD}{\mathcal{O}}_{G/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(G)}@>{}>{}>R\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
{\mathcal{O}}_{T/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(T)}@>{}>{}>K\end{CD}

we need to show that the image of every element of
{\mathcal{O}}_{T/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(T)}
has positive valuation in
K
. By (
4.14
) it suffices to show this for the
\phi
-invariant characters of
T
. Let
\lambda
be any
\phi
-invariant character of
T
and let
V
be a representation of
G
with a nonzero
\lambda
weight-space.
11
11

11

For example, if
w\cdot\lambda
is dominant then we can take
V
to be the irreducible representation with highest weight
w\cdot\lambda
To each weight
\mu
appearing in
V
, we assign the following numbers:

•

Let
n_{\mu}
denote the size of the orbit of
\mu
under the action of
\phi
on the weights of
V
.

•

Let
v_{\mu}
denote
\frac{1}{n_{\mu}}
times the valuation of
\prod_{i=1}^{n_{\mu}}\phi^{i}(\mu)
in
K
.

Let
v_{0}
denote the minimum of
v_{\mu}
as
\mu
ranges over all of the weights in
V
. To show that
v_{\lambda}\geq 0
, it suffices to show that
v_{0}\geq 0
.

To see that
v_{0}\geq 0
, let
r
denote the dimension of the subspace of
V
spanned by the weight spaces
V_{\mu}
with
v_{\mu}=v_{0}
. Because
V
is
\phi
-equivariant, the representation
\Lambda^{r}V
also admits a natural
\phi
-equivariance structure, so we may consider its character
\chi_{\Lambda^{r}V}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{G/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(G)}
. As a
G
-representation,
\Lambda^{r}V
contains a unique
\phi
-invariant weight
\lambda_{0}
with valuation
v_{0}r
, the
\lambda_{0}
-weight space has multiplicity 1, and
v_{0}r
is
strictly
smaller than the valuation of any other
\phi
-invariant weight. By the non-archimedean property of the valuation on
K
, it follows that

\operatorname{val}_{K}(\chi_{\Lambda^{r}V})=\operatorname{val}_{K}(\lambda_{0}).

Because
\chi_{\Lambda^{r}V}
is an element of
{\mathcal{O}}_{G/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(G)}
, the commutativity of (
4.15
) implies that
\operatorname{val}_{K}(\chi_{\Lambda^{r}V})\geq 0
. As a result,

v_{0}r=\operatorname{val}_{K}(\lambda_{0})=\operatorname{val}_{K}(\chi_{\Lambda^{r}V})\geq 0,

so
v_{0}\geq 0
, which is what we needed to show.

4.8.5.
Step 2

G
is connected and
H=T
is an arbitrary subtorus of
G
. Let
M=Z_{G}(T)
denote the Levi subgroup of
G
associated to
T
. It is also preserved by
\phi
. Let
T^{\prime}
denote a maximal torus inside of
M
(which necessarily contains
T
). By modifying
\phi
by conjugation by an element of
M
, we may assume that
\phi
preserves
T^{\prime}
as well. We have a short exact sequence

0\to\Lambda\to X^{*}(T^{\prime})\to X^{*}(T)\to 0

where
\Lambda
is a finitely generated free abelian group. The snake lemma identifies the cokernel of
X^{*}(T^{\prime})^{\phi}\to X^{*}(T)^{\phi}
with a subgroup of
\Lambda_{\phi}
, so the cokernel must be finitely generated. Because the action of
\phi
on
X^{*}(T^{\prime})
has finite order, it follows that the rationalization of the cokernel of
X^{*}(T^{\prime})^{\phi}\to X^{*}(T)^{\phi}
vanishes, so the cokernel itself must be a finite abelian group. This shows that in the diagram

T/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(T)\to T^{\prime}/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(T^{\prime})\to G/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(G)

the first arrow is finite. Since the second arrow is finite by Step 1, this case is finished.

4.8.6.
Step 3

Now suppose that
G
is arbitary and
H=T
is an arbitary subtorus of
G
. Then
T/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(T)\to G/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(G)
factors as

T/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(T)\to G^{\circ}/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(G^{\circ})\to G^{\circ}/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(G)\to G/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(G).

4.8.7.
Step 4

Now suppose that
G
is arbitary and
H
is connected. By modifying
\phi
by conjugation by an element of
H
, we may assume that
\phi
preserves a maximal torus
T
of
H
. By (
4.14
) and highest weight theory for
H
, we see that the map
T/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(T)\to H/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(H)
is dominant. Since

T/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(T)\to G/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(G)

is finite, it follows formally that

H/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(H)\to G/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(G)

is, too.

4.8.8.
Step 5

Now suppose that both
G
and
H
are arbitary. For any
x\in H
, the component
H^{\circ}\cdot x
of
H
is preserved by the

\phi
-twisted adjoint action of
H^{\circ}
, since

h\cdot H^{\circ}\cdot x\cdot\phi(h^{-1})=h\cdot H^{\circ}\cdot\mathrm{Ad}_{x}(\phi(h^{-1}))\cdot x.

This also shows the map

(4.16)

H^{\circ}\cdot x/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(H^{\circ})\to G/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(G)

identifies with

H^{\circ}/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\mathrm{Ad}_{x}\circ\phi}(H^{\circ})\to G/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\mathrm{Ad}_{x}\circ\phi}(G)

so by Step 4 we know that (
4.16
) is finite. Since
H/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(H^{\circ})
is a finite disjoint union of components

H^{\circ}\cdot x/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(H^{\circ})
, it follows that

H/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(H^{\circ})\to G/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(G)

is finite. Since

H/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(H^{\circ})\to H/\!/\mathrm{Ad}_{\phi}(H)

is dominant, the result follows.

∎[Proposition
4.7.4
]

5.
Rationality and independence of
\ell
of the excursion algebra

In this section we show that the excursion algebra
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
is defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
and, in particular,
is independent of
\ell
. This will be based on the “independence of
\ell
” results from the paper
[
Dr
]
.

5.1.
Statement of the result

For the duration of this section we will assume that
X
is smooth, so that we can apply the results of
[
Dr
]
.

5.1.1.

We will prove:

Theorem 5.1.2
.

There exists a canonically defined
{\mathbb{Q}}
-algebra
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})_{\mathbb{Q}}
that
provides a rational model for
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
for any
\ell
. Moreover,
the elements
\operatorname{H}(V,x^{\prime})
for
V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathsf{G}})^{\heartsuit,c}
,
x^{\prime}\in|X|
are rational.

Note that the second assertion in Theorem
5.1.2
means that the homomorphism

{\mathcal{H}}_{X}({\mathsf{G}})\overset{\text{\eqref{e:Hecke Homomorphism}}}{\to}\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})

is defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
.

From here we obtain:

Corollary 5.1.3
.

The algebra
{\mathcal{H}}_{X,{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathsf{G}})
admits a quotient
{\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{glob}}_{X,{\mathbb{Q}}}
such that for any
\mathsf{e}
,
the homomorphism (
4.7
) factors as

{\mathcal{H}}_{X}({\mathsf{G}})=\mathsf{e}\underset{{\mathbb{Q}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{H}}_{X,{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathsf{G}})\to\mathsf{e}\underset{{\mathbb{Q}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{glob}}_{X,{\mathbb{Q}}}\hookrightarrow\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}}).

5.1.4.

Assume for a moment that
{\mathsf{G}}={\mathop{\operatorname{\rm GL}}}_{n}
. Combining Corollary
5.1.3
and Theorem
4.4.2
(b)
we obtain:

Corollary 5.1.5
.

The algebra
{\mathcal{H}}_{X,{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathsf{G}})
admits a quotient
{\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{glob}}_{X,{\mathbb{Q}}}
, such that the homomorphism (
4.7
) factors as

{\mathcal{H}}_{X}({\mathsf{G}})=\mathsf{e}\underset{{\mathbb{Q}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{H}}_{X,{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathsf{G}})\to\mathsf{e}\underset{{\mathbb{Q}}}{\otimes}{\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{glob}}_{X,{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathsf{G}})\simeq\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}}).

Remark 5.1.6
.

Note that Corollary
5.1.5
gives a rather robust characterization of the rational structure on
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
for
{\mathsf{G}}=GL_{n}
; indeed,
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})_{\mathbb{Q}}

is the quotient
{\mathcal{H}}^{\operatorname{glob}}_{X,{\mathbb{Q}}}
of
{\mathcal{H}}_{X,{\mathbb{Q}}}({\mathsf{G}})
.

However, for a general
{\mathsf{G}}
, since an assertion parallel to Theorem
4.4.2
(b) fails, Theorem
5.1.2

is a rather weak statement
12
12

12

It can be called “rationality in name only”.
.

5.1.7.

Let
X
be a curve, and let
\overline{X}
be its compactification. One can show that V. Lafforgue’s construction
(with an extension by
[
Xue
]
from cuspidal to all automorphic functions plus an input from
[
AGKRRV
]
)
can be upgraded to
an action of
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
on the space

\operatorname{Autom}:=\operatorname{Funct}_{c}(\operatorname{Bun}_{G}^{\operatorname{level}}({\mathbb{F}}_{q}),\mathsf{e}),

where:

•

G
is the Langlands dual of
{\mathsf{G}}
;

•

\operatorname{Bun}_{G}^{\operatorname{level}}
is the moduli space of
G
-bundles on
\overline{X}
with level structure on
\overline{X}-X
, whose
depth depends on the amount of ramification we allow for our choice of
\operatorname{QLisse}(X)
(see Sect.
4.1.2
).

Following
[
VLaf
, Conjecture 12.12]
we propose:

Conjecture 5.1.8
.

The action of
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})
on
\operatorname{Autom}
is compatible with the rational structures.

We do not know how to approach this conjecture for an arbitrary
{\mathsf{G}}
(see Remark
5.1.6
),
since, on the one hand, Theorem
5.1.2
does not give a unique characterization of the rational
structure, and on the other hand, its proof is rather inexplicit (yet, at the end of the day, it follows from
Drinfeld’s theorem in
[
Dr
]
, which is deduced by comparison with the automorphic side).

However, we claim:

Theorem 5.1.9
.

The statement of Conjecture
5.1.8
holds for
{\mathsf{G}}=GL_{n}
.

Proof.

Follows from the fact that the action of
{\mathcal{H}}_{X}({\mathsf{G}})
on
\operatorname{Autom}
is compatible with the rational structures.

∎

Remark 5.1.10
.

An assertion parallel to Conjecture
5.1.8
holds in the context of geometrization of the local Langlands correspondence
(à la Fargues-Scholze). This has been established in
[
Sch
]
using motivic methods.

One of the key points of this
paper is that when studying motivic local systems in the local situation, it is sufficient to consider
mixed Tate

motives, for which one knows the existence of the t-structure, etc. We do not know how to adopt these methods to
the context of a global curve.

5.2.
The pro-reductive quotient of the Weil group

The rest of this section is devoted to the proof of Theorem
5.1.2
.

5.2.1.

Recall the group
\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)
, see (
3.15
). Note that the subgroup

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{unip}}(X)\subset\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)\subset\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)

is normal. Consider the short exact sequence

(5.1)

1\to\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red}}(X)\to\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)/\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{unip}}(X)\to{\mathbb{Z}}\to 1.

Let us denote by
\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{red}}(X)
the push-out of (
5.1
) with respect to

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red}}(X)\to\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red,relev}}(X),

i.e.,

(5.2)

1\to\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red,relev}}(X)\to\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{red}}(X)\to{\mathbb{Z}}\to 1.

The group
\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{red}}(X)
comes equipped with a homomorphism

(5.3)

\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X)\to\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{alg}}(X)\to\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{red}}(X).

5.2.2.

By Theorem
3.7.2
, for the proof of Theorem
5.1.2
, we can replace

\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{0}:=\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)}).

Note that the stack
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
can be identified with

\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{red}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}),

see Sect.
3.6.4
.

Hence, we can interpret
\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{0}
as

\Gamma(\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{red}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}),{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{red}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})}).

5.2.3.

Let
\operatorname{Gal-Drinf}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)
be the pro-semi-simple group over
\mathsf{e}
defined in
[
Dr
, Sect. 1.1.2]
(in
loc. cit.
it is denoted

\hat{\Pi}_{\lambda}
).
It comes equipped with a map

\operatorname{Gal-Drinf}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)\to\widehat{\mathbb{Z}},

where the pro-finite group
\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}
is viewed as a pro-semi-simple group.

Denote

\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X):=\operatorname{Gal-Drinf}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)\underset{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}}{\times}{\mathbb{Z}}.

By construction, the group
\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X)
comes equipped with a homomorphism

(5.4)

\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X)\to\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X).

5.2.4.

We claim:

Proposition-Construction 5.2.5
.

There is a canonical isomorphism

\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{red}}(X)\simeq\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X),

which makes the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X)@>{\operatorname{id}}>{}>\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X)\\
@V{\text{\eqref{e:discr to Drinf'}}}V{}V@V{}V{\text{\eqref{e:discr to Drinf}}}V\\
\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{red}}(X)@>{\sim}>{}>\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X)\end{CD}

commute.

The proposition will be proved in Sect.
5.3
.

5.2.6.

By Proposition
5.2.5
, we have:

(5.5)

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\simeq\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}),

compatibly with maps to

\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}).

Therefore, for the proof of Theorem
5.1.2
, we can further replace

\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{0}\rightsquigarrow\operatorname{Exc-Drinf}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{0}:=\\
=\Gamma(\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}),{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})}).

5.3.
Proof of Proposition
5.2.5

5.3.1.

We will construct an equivalence of symmetric monoidal categories

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X))\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{red}}(X)),

compatible with the forgetful functors to
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X))
.

5.3.2.

Let

\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,ss},0}(X)^{\heartsuit}\subset\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,0}}(X)^{\heartsuit}

be the full
abelian
subcategory consisting of semi-simple objects, and let

\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,ss},0}(X):=D(\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,ss},0}(X)^{\heartsuit}).

5.3.3.

Let

\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,ss,fin.det}}(X)\subset\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,ss},0}(X)

be the full subcategory, we impose the further condition that the irreducible constituents are
local systems with a finite order determinant.

Unwinding, we obtain:

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X))\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}\underset{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}}{\otimes}\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,ss,fin.det}}(X),

where we note that

\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}\simeq\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,ss,fin.det}}(\operatorname{pt}).

5.3.4.

Note, however, that the naturally defined functor

{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{ss},0}\underset{\hat{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}}{\otimes}\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,ss,fin.det}}(X)\to\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,ss},0}(X)

is an equivalence,
where

{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{ss},0}:=\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,ss},0}(\operatorname{pt}).

Hence, we can rewrite

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X))\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}\underset{{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{ss},0}}{\otimes}\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,ss},0}(X).

5.3.5.

Note that we have:

(5.6)

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{red}}(X))\simeq({\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red,relev}}(X)))^{\operatorname{Frob}}\overset{\text{Proposition~\ref{p:Gal red}}}{\simeq}\\
\simeq({\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red,relev},0}(X)))^{\operatorname{Frob}}\simeq(\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X))^{\operatorname{Frob}}\overset{\text{\eqref{e:strange category}}}{\simeq}{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}\underset{{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}}{\otimes}\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X).

Hence, in order to prove Proposition
5.2.5
, it remains to establish an equivalence

(5.7)

{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}\underset{{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{ss},0}}{\otimes}\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,ss},0}(X)\simeq\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X),

as symmetric monoidal categories, compatibly with the forgetful functors to
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X))
.

5.3.6.

Note that we have a canonically defined functor
13
13

13

Note that the functor (
5.8
) is no longer fully faithful.

(5.8)

\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,ss},0}(X)\to\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X).

The functor (
5.8
) gives rise to a functor
\to
in (
5.7
). Let us show that the latter
is an equivalence.

By Corollary
2.1.11
, it suffices to show that the induced functor

(5.9)

\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{ss},0}}{\otimes}\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,ss},0}(X)\simeq\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}}{\otimes}{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}\underset{{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{ss},0}}{\otimes}\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,ss},0}(X)\to\\
\to\operatorname{Vect}_{\mathsf{e}}\underset{{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{0}}{\otimes}\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil},0}(X)\simeq\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{relev},0}(X)

is an equivalence.

However, the latter is obvious: both sides of (
5.9
) are semi-simple, and the functor
(
5.9
) induces a bijection on the set of isomorphism classes of objects.

∎[Proposition
5.2.5
]

5.4.
Interlude: the “motivic” version

In this subsection we will comment on the relation between the Galois groups appearing above and another object from
[
Dr
]
.

5.4.1.

Let
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red,arithm}}(X)
be the semi-simple envelope of the arithmetic Galois group of
X
, i.e.,

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red,arithm}}(X):=\operatorname{Gal-Drinf}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X).

We have a short exact sequence

1\to\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red,relev}}(X)\to\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red,arithm}}(X)\to\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}\to 1.

5.4.2.

Consider the pullback

(5.10)

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{mot}}(X):=\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red,arithm}}(X)\underset{\widehat{\mathbb{Z}}}{\times}{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{red,wt}},

where
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{red,wt}}
is the maximal reductive quotient of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg,wt}}
. We have

\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{red}}(X)\simeq\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{mot}}(X)\underset{{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{red,wt}}}{\times}{\mathbb{Z}}.

Then
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{mot}}(X)
is isomorphic to the group denoted by
\widehat{\Pi}^{\operatorname{mot}}_{\lambda}
in
[
Dr
, Sect. 6.1]
.

Note that we have:

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{mot}}(X))\simeq{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{red,wt}})\underset{{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{red,0}})}{\otimes}\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,ss},0}(X),

where

{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{red,0}})\simeq{\mathbb{Z}}{\operatorname{-mod}}_{\operatorname{ss},0}.

5.4.3.

Consider the stack

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{mot}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X):=\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{mot}}(X),{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}).

Note, however, that since
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{mot}}(X)
is pro-reductive, the stack
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{mot}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
is the union
of (infinitely many) connected components of the form
\operatorname{pt}/H
, where
H
is reductive group.

For every finite sub-union

{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{mot}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\subset\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{mot}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),

set

{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{\operatorname{mot}}:=\Gamma({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{mot}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{mot}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)})

and

{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{mot,coarse}}(X):=\operatorname{Spec}({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{\operatorname{mot}}).

Set

\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{mot,coarse}}(X):=\cup\,^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{mot,coarse}}(X)

Thus,
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{mot,coarse}}(X)
is (an infinite) union of point-schemes.

Remark 5.4.4
.

The above definition of
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{mot,coarse}}(X)
coincides with one in
[
Dr
, Sect. 6.6]
.

5.4.5.

The homomorphism

\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{red}}(X)\to\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{mot}}(X)

gives rise to a map

(5.11)

\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{mot}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)\to\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X).

This map is a closed embedding when restricted to every finite union
{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{mot}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)
.

This map in turns gives rise to a surjection

\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})\simeq\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{0}=\Gamma(\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)})\to\\
\to\Gamma({}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{mot}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X),{\mathcal{O}}_{{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{mot}}_{\mathsf{G}}(X)})={}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{\operatorname{mot}},

and hence to a closed embedding

{}^{\prime}\!\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{mot,coarse}}(X)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}(X).

These embeddings combine to a map

(5.12)

\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{mot,coarse}}(X)\hookrightarrow\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}(X).

The image of (
5.12
) is the (disjoint) union of
\mathsf{e}
-points of
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}(X)

that correspond to those semi-simple Weil
{\mathsf{G}}
-local systems on
X
that are
weakly motivic
in sense of
[
Dr
, Proposition 6.2.1]
, i.e.
for every representation
{\mathsf{G}}\to GL_{n}
, the corresponding object of
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,loc.fin}}(X)^{\heartsuit}

belongs to
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,wt}}(X)^{\heartsuit}
.

Remark 5.4.6
.

The image for the terminology “weakly motivic” is the following:

By
[
LLaf
]
, when
X
is a curve, every irreducible object in
\operatorname{QLisse}^{\operatorname{Weil,ss,fin.det}}(X)^{\heartsuit}
,
up to a half-integral Tate twist, is a direct summand of a local system that appears in the cohomology of a smooth and proper
direct image
Y\to X
of the constant sheaf on
Y
.

A further modification of the action of the Frobenius by a Weil number stays motivic, by Honda’s theorem.

5.4.7.

Once Theorem
5.1.2
is proved, the same argument shows that the (ind)-affine scheme
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{mot,coarse}}(X)
is also defined
over
{\mathbb{Q}}
, as well as the map (
5.12
).

The image of the map (
5.12
) is called the locus of
motivic
local systems.

Remark 5.4.8
.

If in (
5.10
) instead of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{red,wt}}
we took
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{red}}

(the reductive quotient of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{alg}}
), we would obtain a moduli stack, whose
coarse moduli space is still a disjoint union of points, and its image in
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}(X)

is now
all

\mathsf{e}
-points of
\operatorname{LS}_{\mathsf{G}}^{\operatorname{arithm,coarse}}(X)
.

Note, however, that an analog of Theorem
5.1.2
does
not
apply here, since
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{red}}
, unlike
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{red,wt}}
,
is
not
defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
.

Remark 5.4.9
.

An intermediate option is, instead of
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{red,wt}}
or
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{red}}
, to take the diagonalizable group,
whose characters are
integral units
in
\mathsf{e}
. In this way, we obtain the pro-reductive group that is denoted
\widehat{\Pi}^{\operatorname{red}}_{\lambda}
in

[
Dr
, Sect. 3.5]
.

Note the resulting coarse moduli space is still
not
defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
, for the same reason as for
{\mathbb{Z}}^{\operatorname{red}}
.

5.5.
Drinfeld’s category

5.5.1.

For a field
{{\mathbf{k}}}
of characteristic
0
, let
\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}({{\mathbf{k}}})
be the category of affine group-schemes
(a.k.a., pro-algebraic groups) over
{{\mathbf{k}}}
.

Let
\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})
denote the (ordinary) category of group schemes over
{{\mathbf{k}}}
“up to conjugation by their neutral components”.
So the objects of
\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}^{\mathrm{coarse}}(k)
are the same as those of
\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}({{\mathbf{k}}})
, and

\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})}(G,H)=\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})}(G,H)/H^{\circ}({{\mathbf{k}}})

where an element
h\in H^{\circ}({{\mathbf{k}}})
acts on
\operatorname{Hom}_{\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}({{\mathbf{k}}})}(G,H)
by

f\mapsto\mathrm{Ad}_{h}\circ f.

5.5.2.

Let

\operatorname{Pro-ss}({{\mathbf{k}}})\subset\operatorname{Pro-red}({{\mathbf{k}}})\subset\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}({{\mathbf{k}}})

and

\operatorname{Pro-ss}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})\subset\operatorname{Pro-red}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})\subset\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})

be the full subcategories, where we restrict objects to be pro-semi simple (resp., pro-reductive)
algebraic groups.

Note that
\operatorname{Pro-ss}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})
and
\operatorname{Pro-red}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})
are the same categories that
have been introduced in
[
Dr
, Sect. 1.2.3]
(NB: in
loc. cit.
they are denoted
\operatorname{Pro-ss}({{\mathbf{k}}})

and
\operatorname{Pro-red}({{\mathbf{k}}})
, respectively).

In this subsection we will only need the underlying groupoids of
\operatorname{Pro-ss}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})
and
\operatorname{Pro-red}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})
,
which we denote by the same symbol, by a slight abuse of notation.

5.5.3.

Let
\operatorname{Pro-red-ext}^{\operatorname{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})
be a similarly defined groupoid, where we consider extensions

(5.13)

1\to H\to H^{\prime}\to{\mathbb{Z}}\to 1,

where
H
is a pro-reductive group.

5.5.4.

As is explained in
[
Dr
]
, to an object
H\in\operatorname{Pro-red}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})
, we can attach a well-defined scheme

H/\!/\operatorname{Ad}(H^{\circ}),

where
H^{\circ}
is the connected component of
H
.

Similarly, to an object
H^{\prime}\in\operatorname{Pro-red-ext}^{\operatorname{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})
, we can attach a well-defined scheme

H^{\prime}/\!/\operatorname{Ad}(H^{\circ}).

5.5.5.

Consider the functor from
\operatorname{Pro-red-ext}({{\mathbf{k}}})
to the category of (possibly infinite unions of) affine schemes

(5.14)

H^{\prime}\mapsto\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(H^{\prime},{\mathsf{G}})/\!/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}),

Lemma 5.5.6
.

The functor (
5.14
) factors via a functor

\operatorname{Pro-red-ext}^{\operatorname{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})\to\operatorname{Sch}^{\operatorname{aff}}_{/{{\mathbf{k}}}}.

Corollary 5.5.7
.

The functor

(5.15)

H^{\prime}\mapsto\operatorname{Exc}(H^{\prime},{\mathsf{G}}):=\Gamma(\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(H^{\prime},{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}}),{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(H^{\prime},{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})})

from the category of extensions (
5.13
) to
\operatorname{ComAlg}(\operatorname{Vect}_{{{\mathbf{k}}}})
factors via a functor

(5.16)

\operatorname{Pro-red-ext}^{\operatorname{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})\to\operatorname{ComAlg}(\operatorname{Vect}_{{{\mathbf{k}}}}).

Remark 5.5.8
.

Note that the functor

H^{\prime}\mapsto\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(H^{\prime},{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})

with values in stacks does
not
factor via
\operatorname{Pro-red-ext}^{\operatorname{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})
.

5.5.9.

Recall now that according to
[
Dr
, Sect. 1.2.3]
, for a map of algebraically closed fields
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{1}\to{{\mathbf{k}}}_{2}
,
the functor

\operatorname{Pro-red-ext}^{\operatorname{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Pro-red-ext}^{\operatorname{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}}_{2})

is an equivalence.

In particular, the groupoid
\operatorname{Pro-red-ext}^{\operatorname{coarse}}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})
carries a canonical action of
\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{\mathbb{Q}})
. Hence,
for an object of
\operatorname{Pro-red-ext}^{\operatorname{coarse}}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})
, it makes sense to talk about structure of
\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{\mathbb{Q}})
-equivariance
on it.

5.5.10.

Assume now that
X
is smooth. We now recall the main result of the paper
[
Dr
]
, namely, Theorem 1.4.1 in
loc. cit.
:

Theorem 5.5.11
.

There exists a canonically defined object
\operatorname{Gal-Drinf}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}}\in\operatorname{Pro-red}^{\operatorname{coarse}}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})
, equipped with a structure of

\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{\mathbb{Q}})
-equivariance, such that for any
\ell\neq\operatorname{char}(k)
and an embedding

\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\to\mathsf{e}
,
the image of
\operatorname{Gal-Drinf}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}}
in
\operatorname{Pro-red}^{\operatorname{coarse}}(\mathsf{e})
identifies with the class of the extension

\operatorname{Gal-Drinf}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)
.

As a formal corollary, we obtain:

Corollary 5.5.12
.

There exists an object
\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X)_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}}\in\operatorname{Pro-red-ext}^{\operatorname{coarse}}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}})
, equipped with a structure of

\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{\mathbb{Q}})
-equivariance, such that for any
\ell\neq\operatorname{char}(k)
and an embedding
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\to\mathsf{e}
,
the image of
\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X)_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}}
in
\operatorname{Pro-red-ext}^{\operatorname{coarse}}(\mathsf{e})
identifies with the class of the extension

\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X)
.

5.5.13.

Let
x^{\prime}
be a closed point of
X
, and let
\operatorname{Frob}_{x^{\prime}}\in\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X)
be the conjugacy class
of the corresponding Frobenius element. Let
\overline{\operatorname{Frob}}_{x^{\prime},\mathsf{e}}
denote the image of
\operatorname{Frob}_{x^{\prime}}
under the composition

\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X)/\operatorname{Ad}(\operatorname{Weil}^{\operatorname{discr}}(X))\to\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X)/\operatorname{Ad}(\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X))\to\\
\to\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X)/\!/\operatorname{Ad}(\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X)).

5.5.14.

Another assertion of
[
Dr
, Theorem 1.4.1]
reads:

Theorem 5.5.15
.

For any
x^{\prime}
as above, there exists a
\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{\mathbb{Q}})
-invariant element

\overline{\operatorname{Frob}}_{x^{\prime},\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}}\in\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X)_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}}/\!/\operatorname{Ad}(\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X)_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}})

such that for any embedding
\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}\to\mathsf{e}
its image under

\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X)_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}}/\!/\operatorname{Ad}(\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X)_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}})\to\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X)/\!/\operatorname{Ad}(\operatorname{Weil-Drinf}(X))

equals
\overline{\operatorname{Frob}}_{x^{\prime},\mathsf{e}}
.

5.6.
Proof of Theorem
5.1.2

5.6.1.

Note that for a map of algebraically closed fields
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{1}\to{{\mathbf{k}}}_{2}
, the diagram

\begin{CD}\operatorname{Pro-red-ext}^{\operatorname{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}}_{1})@>{\text{\eqref{e:coarse fact}}}>{}>\operatorname{ComAlg}(\operatorname{Vect}_{{{\mathbf{k}}}_{1}})\\
@V{}V{}V@V{}V{}V\\
\operatorname{Pro-red-ext}^{\operatorname{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}}_{2})@>{\text{\eqref{e:coarse fact}}}>{}>\operatorname{ComAlg}(\operatorname{Vect}_{{{\mathbf{k}}}_{2}})\end{CD}

commutes.

Combined with Corollary
5.5.12
, we obtain that

\operatorname{Exc-Drinf}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{0}_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}}\in\operatorname{ComAlg}(\operatorname{Vect}_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}}^{\heartsuit})

carries a structure of
\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{\mathbb{Q}})
-equivariance.

5.6.2.

Furthermore, for a representation
V
of
{\mathsf{G}}
defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
, the natural transformation of functors

(H\mapsto H^{\prime}/\!/\operatorname{Ad}(H^{\circ}))\Rightarrow(H\mapsto\operatorname{Exc}(H^{\prime},{\mathsf{G}})),

given by

H^{\prime}/\!/\operatorname{Ad}(H^{\circ})\times\mathbf{Maps}_{\operatorname{groups}}(H^{\prime},{\mathsf{G}})/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})\to{\mathsf{G}}/\!/\operatorname{Ad}({\mathsf{G}})\overset{\operatorname{Tr(-,V)}}{\to}{\mathbb{A}}^{1}

is also compatible with maps between algebraically closed fields.

Combined with Theorem
5.5.15
, this implies that for any
x^{\prime}\in X
, and
V\in{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}({\mathsf{G}})^{\heartsuit,c}
defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
,
the image of the element
\operatorname{H}(V,x^{\prime})
under

\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})\to\operatorname{Exc}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{0}\simeq\operatorname{Exc-Drinf}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{0}

comes from a
\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{\mathbb{Q}})
-invariant element in
\operatorname{Exc-Drinf}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{0}_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}}
.

5.6.3.

Thus, we obtain that in order to prove Theorem
5.1.2
, it remains to establish the following:

Theorem 5.6.4
.

The action of
\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{\mathbb{Q}})
on
\operatorname{Exc-Drinf}(X,{\mathsf{G}})^{0}_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}}
is continuous.

The theorem will be proved in Sect.
5.7.8
.

5.6.5.

Assume for a moment that
{\mathsf{G}}=GL_{n}
. Then we claim that the statement of Theorem
5.6.4
is automatic:

Indeed, this follows from Theorem
4.4.2
(b) and the fact that the elements
\operatorname{H}(V,x^{\prime})
are

\operatorname{Gal}(\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}/{\mathbb{Q}})
-invariant.

5.7.
The continuity theorem–preparations

5.7.1.

A map of fields
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{1}\to{{\mathbf{k}}}_{2}
induces a base-change functor
\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}({{\mathbf{k}}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}({{\mathbf{k}}}_{2})
, which in turn
descends to a functor
\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}}_{1})\to\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}}_{2})
.
In particular, taking
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{1}={{\mathbf{k}}}_{2}={{\mathbf{k}}}
, we see that
\operatorname{Aut}({{\mathbf{k}}})
(viewed as an abstract group) acts on
\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})
.

If
\sigma
is an element
of
\operatorname{Aut}({{\mathbf{k}}})
we will denote the corresponding automorphism of
\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})
by
F_{\sigma}
. Note that any
\operatorname{Aut}({{\mathbf{k}}})
-equivariant object of

\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}({{\mathbf{k}}})
defines an
\operatorname{Aut}({{\mathbf{k}}})
-equivariant object of
\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})
.

5.7.2.

Note that when
{{\mathbf{k}}}
is algebraically closed,
[
Dr
, Sect. 2.2.2]
gives an explicit description of (the underlying groupoid of)
\textrm{Pro-ss}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})
in terms of (pro-)combinatorial data.

In terms of the notation of
loc. cit.
, suppose we are given some object
G
of
\textrm{Pro-ss}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})
and some field
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{0}\subseteq{{\mathbf{k}}}
such that the map

Z_{\Delta}({{\mathbf{k}}})\to Z

is
\operatorname{Aut}({{\mathbf{k}}}/{{\mathbf{k}}}_{0})
-equivariant (where
Z
is given the trivial
\operatorname{Aut}({{\mathbf{k}}}/{{\mathbf{k}}}_{0})
-action). Then the construction of
[
Dr
, Sect. 2.2.4]

produces a pro-semisimple group
G_{0}
defined over
{{\mathbf{k}}}_{0}
whose base-change to
{{\mathbf{k}}}
is isomorphic to
G
.
This endows
G
with
\operatorname{Aut}({{\mathbf{k}}}/{{\mathbf{k}}}_{0})
-equivariance data which we call “split” equivariance data for
G
.

5.7.3.

Since
GL_{n}
is defined over
{\mathbb{Q}}
, it acquires equivariance data for
\operatorname{Aut}({{\mathbf{k}}})
as an object of

\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})
. As a result, if
G
is any
\operatorname{Aut}({{\mathbf{k}}})
-equivariant object of
\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})
, we obtain an action of
\operatorname{Aut}({{\mathbf{k}}})
on

K^{+}(G)
, where the latter is the Grothendieck semi-ring of the small abelian category
{\mathop{\operatorname{\rm Rep}}}(G)^{\heartsuit,c}
.

Namely, if
\sigma
is an element of
\operatorname{Aut}({{\mathbf{k}}})
and
\rho\in K^{+}(G)
then
\sigma\cdot\rho
is the isomorphism class of the representation

G\xrightarrow{\sim}F_{\sigma}(G)\xrightarrow{F_{\sigma}(\rho)}F_{\sigma}(GL_{n})\xrightarrow{\sim}GL_{n}.

5.7.4.

There is another piece of data that we can extract from
\operatorname{Aut}({{\mathbf{k}}})
-equivariance for
G
:

If
\sigma
is an element of
\operatorname{Aut}({{\mathbf{k}}})
, then (for any
G
) there is a natural map

(G/\!/\operatorname{Ad}(G^{\circ}))({{\mathbf{k}}})\to(F_{\sigma}(G)/\!/\operatorname{Ad}(F_{\sigma}(G)^{\circ}))({{\mathbf{k}}}).

Hence, if
G
is
\operatorname{Aut}({{\mathbf{k}}})
-equivariant then we obtain an action of
\operatorname{Aut}({{\mathbf{k}}})
on
(G/\!/\operatorname{Ad}(G^{\circ}))({{\mathbf{k}}})
.

5.7.5.

These two pieces of data are compatible in the following way: if
G
is any object of
\operatorname{Pro-AlgGrp}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})
, there is a well-defined trace map

\operatorname{tr}:(G/\!/\operatorname{Ad}(G^{\circ}))({{\mathbf{k}}})\times K^{+}(G)\to{{\mathbf{k}}}

and if
G
is equipped with the data of
\operatorname{Aut}({{\mathbf{k}}})
-equivariance, then the trace map intertwines the induced action of
\operatorname{Aut}({{\mathbf{k}}})
on
(G/\!/\operatorname{Ad}(G^{\circ}))({{\mathbf{k}}})\times K^{+}(G)
with the canonical action of
\operatorname{Aut}({{\mathbf{k}}})
on
{{\mathbf{k}}}
.

5.7.6.

According to
[
Dr
, Sect. 1.4.2]
, the object
G=\operatorname{Gal-Drinf}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}}
of
\textrm{Pro-ss}^{\mathrm{coarse}}(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
admits an equivariance datum for
\operatorname{Gal}({\mathbb{Q}})
, which is uniquely characterized by the requirement that the Frobenius elements in
(G/\!/\operatorname{Ad}(G^{\circ}))(\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
are fixed by the action of
\operatorname{Gal}({\mathbb{Q}})
.

This has the following representation-theoretic consequence: if
\operatorname{Frob}_{x}
is any Frobenius element in
G
and
\rho:G\to GL(V)
is any element of
K^{+}(G)
, then

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}_{x},V_{\sigma})=\sigma\cdot\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}_{x},V)

where
V_{\sigma}
is the element of
K^{+}(G)
obtained by applying the action from Sect.
5.7.3
.

Proposition 5.7.7
.

Suppose that
H
is a finite-type quotient of
\operatorname{Gal-Drinf}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}}
. Then there is an open subgroup
U\subseteq\operatorname{Gal}({\mathbb{Q}})
such that

•

H
has a split model over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}^{U}
.

•

The map
\operatorname{Gal-Drinf}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)_{\overline{{\mathbb{Q}}}}\to H
is
U
-equivariant, where
H
is equipped with the split equivariance structure.

5.7.8.

The proof of Proposition
5.7.7
will occupy the next section of this paper. Taking the proposition for granted, we will now prove Theorem
5.6.4
.

Let
\sigma:\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red},\operatorname{relev}}(X)\to\mathsf{G}
be a semisimple
\mathsf{G}
local system on
X
, and assume that
\sigma
admits a Weil structure.
Pick a faithful representation
\mathsf{G}\to GL_{n}
. It is well known that there is an etale local system
\sigma^{\prime}:\operatorname{Gal-Drinf}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)\to GL_{n}
whose restriction to
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red},\operatorname{relev}}(X)
agrees with our original composite

\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red},\operatorname{relev}}(X)\to\mathsf{G}\to GL_{n}.

Let
H
denote the image of
\sigma^{\prime}
. By Proposition
5.7.7
, we know that there is an open subgroup
U
of
\operatorname{Gal}({\mathbb{Q}})
such that
\operatorname{Gal-Drinf}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)\to H
is
U
-equivariant (where
H
has the split equivariance structure).

Let
K
denote the intersection of
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red},\operatorname{relev}}(X)
with the kernel of
\operatorname{Gal-Drinf}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)\to H
. This group is evidently preserved by the action of
U
. Additionally, because
\mathsf{G}\to GL_{n}
was chosen to be injective, we know that
K=\ker(\sigma)
.

Let
H^{\mathrm{geom}}
denote the quotient
\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red},\operatorname{relev}}(X)/K
. We obtain a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Gal}^{\operatorname{red},\operatorname{relev}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Gal-Drinf}^{\operatorname{arithm}}(X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hat{\mathbb{Z}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\mathrm{geom}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Z}/n}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}

where every map is
U
-equivariant. In particular, the group
H\underset{\mathbb{Z}/n}{\times}\mathbb{Z}
acquires a natural (split)
U
-equivariance structure, for which the map

\textrm{Weil-Drinf}(X)\to H\underset{\mathbb{Z}/n}{\times}\mathbb{Z}

is
U
-equivariant.

Since
K
is the kernel of
\sigma
, any map
\textrm{Weil-Drinf}(X)\to\mathsf{G}
extending
\sigma
factors through

\textrm{Weil-Drinf}(X)/K\simeq H\underset{\mathbb{Z}/n}{\times}\mathbb{Z}.

This implies that the restriction map

(5.17)

\operatorname{Maps}(\textrm{Weil-Drinf}(X)/K,\mathsf{G})/\mathsf{G}\to\operatorname{Maps}(\textrm{Weil-Drinf}(X),\mathsf{G})/\mathsf{G}

induces an isomorphism on the component of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}
corresponding to
\sigma
.

Since the map (
5.17
) is
U
-equivariant and the
U
-equivariance data for
\textrm{Weil-Drinf}(X)/K
is split, it follows that the action of
U
on the ring of functions of this component is continuous.

By
[
EK
]
,
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}
only has finitely many connected components, so there is some open subgroup of
\operatorname{Gal}({\mathbb{Q}})
which acts continuously on the whole ring of functions of
\operatorname{LS}^{\operatorname{arithm},0}
. But this implies that the
\operatorname{Gal}({\mathbb{Q}})
action is itself continuous.

∎[Theorem
5.6.4
]

5.8.
Proof of Proposition
5.7.7

5.8.1.

We start with the following observation:

Lemma 5.8.2
.

Let
{{\mathbf{k}}}
be algebraically closed, and let
f_{1},f_{2}:G\to H
be surjective maps of pro-semisimple groups over
{{\mathbf{k}}}
. Assume that:

•

The neutral component of
H
is a product of almost simple groups;

•

f_{1}
and
f_{2}
induce the same map

\pi_{0}(G)\to\pi_{0}(H);

•

For every open subgroup
\Gamma_{0}\subseteq\pi_{0}(H)
, they induce the same map

K^{+}(H\underset{\pi_{0}(H)}{\times}\Gamma_{0})\to K^{+}(G\underset{\pi_{0}(H)}{\times}\Gamma_{0}).

Then
f_{1}=f_{2}
as morphisms in
\textrm{Pro-ss}^{\mathrm{coarse}}({{\mathbf{k}}})
.

Proof.

Because
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
induce the same map
K^{+}(H)\to K^{+}(G)
, we know by
[
Dr
, Lemma 4.1.1(i)]
that
\ker(f_{1})=\ker(f_{2})
; denote this group by
K
.
Then
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
descend to two isomorphisms
G/K\to H
which induce the same map on
\pi_{0}
.

Since the image of
K
in
\pi_{0}(H)
is trivial, we know that for every open subgroup
\Gamma_{0}\subseteq\pi_{0}(H)
,

K\subseteq G\underset{\pi_{0}(H)}{\times}\Gamma_{0}

and so

G/K\underset{\pi_{0}(H)}{\times}\Gamma_{0}\simeq(G\underset{\pi_{0}(H)}{\times}\Gamma_{0})/K.

This implies that
K^{+}(G/K\underset{\pi_{0}(H)}{\times}\Gamma_{0})
is a subring of
K^{+}(G\underset{\pi_{0}(H)}{\times}\Gamma_{0})
,
so by assumption
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
induce the same map

K^{+}(H\underset{\pi_{0}(H)}{\times}\Gamma_{0})\to K^{+}(G/K\underset{\pi_{0}(H)}{\times}\Gamma_{0}).

Now the result follows by
[
Dr
, Theorem 4.3.7(i)]
.
∎

5.8.3.
Step 1

In this step we will prove Proposition
5.7.7
under the additional assumption that
H^{\circ}
is simply connected.

Since
H
is finite-type over
\overline{\mathbb{Q}}
