, there is an open subgroup
U
of
\operatorname{Gal}({\mathbb{Q}})
such that
H
admits a split model over the fixed field of
U
. We claim that after passing to a further open subgroup
V
of
U
the map
\pi:G\to H
becomes
V
-equivariant (where
H
is equipped with the split equivariance datum).

To see this, we will invoke Lemma
5.8.2
. For an element
\sigma\in U
, let
\pi^{\prime}:G\to H
denote the composite

G\xrightarrow{\sim}F_{\sigma}(G)\xrightarrow{F_{\sigma}(\pi)}F_{\sigma}(H)\xrightarrow{\sim}H.

It follows from Chebotarev density and the defining property of the
\operatorname{Gal}({\mathbb{Q}})
-equivariance data on
G
that
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
induce the same map
\pi_{0}(G)\to\pi_{0}(H)
.

For a subgroup
\Gamma_{0}\subseteq\pi_{0}(H)
, we will denote by the same symbols
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
the corresponding maps

G\underset{\pi_{0}(H)}{\times}\Gamma_{0}\to H\underset{\pi_{0}(H)}{\times}\Gamma_{0}.

By Lemma
5.8.2
it remains to show that there is an open subgroup
V
of
U
such that for all
\Gamma_{0}\subseteq\pi_{0}(H)
, the maps

K^{+}(H\underset{\pi_{0}(H)}{\times}\Gamma_{0})\to K^{+}(G\underset{\pi_{0}(H)}{\times}\Gamma_{0})

induced by
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
agree for all
\sigma\in V
. Because
\pi_{0}(H)
is finite, it suffices to work with a single
\Gamma_{0}
. For ease of notation, let
H_{0}
denote
H\underset{\pi_{0}(H)}{\times}\Gamma_{0}
and let
G_{0}
denote
G\underset{\pi_{0}(H)}{\times}\Gamma_{0}
.

By our choice of equivariance data for
H
, for any
\rho:H_{0}\to GL_{n}
, the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\sigma}(H)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\sigma}(GL_{n})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces GL_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{\sigma}(\rho)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\rho}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

commutes for any
\sigma
in an open subgroup of
U
14
14

14

Because
H_{0}
and
GL_{n}
are both finite type groups over the fixed field of
U
, any map
\rho
between them is defined over a finite extension of this fixed field.
. It follows that
\rho\circ\pi^{\prime}=\sigma\cdot(\rho\circ\pi)
in
K^{+}(G_{0})
. In other words, if we write
V
for the class of
\rho\circ\pi
in
K^{+}(G_{0})
, we know that the class of
\rho\circ\pi^{\prime}
is equal to
V_{\sigma}
(using the notation of Sect.
5.7.6
).

Now we note that since
\pi_{0}(G)\underset{\pi_{0}(H)}{\times}\Gamma_{0}
is an open subgroup of
\pi_{0}(G)=\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(X)
, there is a finite cover
\widetilde{X}
of
X
such that

\pi_{0}(G)\underset{\pi_{0}(H)}{\times}\Gamma_{0}\simeq\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\widetilde{X}).

By
[
Dr
, Lemma 3.1.2]
,
G_{0}
identifies
\operatorname{Gal}({\mathbb{Q}})
-equivariantly with the pro-semisimple completion of
\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\widetilde{X})
. Since the trace map
K^{+}(G_{0})\to\operatorname{Fun}(\pi_{1}^{\mathrm{\acute{e}t}}(\widetilde{X})_{\operatorname{Frob}};\overline{\mathbb{Q}})
is injective, to prove that
V=V_{\sigma}
it suffices to check that

\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}_{x},V)=\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}_{x},V_{\sigma})=\sigma\cdot\operatorname{Tr}(\operatorname{Frob}_{x},V)

for all
x\in|\widetilde{X}|
.

For any fixed
\rho
,
[
De3
, Theorem 3.1]
shows that this equality holds for any
\sigma
in an open subgroup of
U
.

Since
H_{0}
is finite-type,
K(H_{0})
is finitely generated, so to check that
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
induce the same map
K^{+}(H_{0})\to K^{+}(G_{0})
it suffices to check this condition for finitely many
\rho
.

5.8.4.

We now wish to relax the assumption that
H^{\circ}
is simply connected. To this end, we will prove the following lemma:

Lemma 5.8.5
.

Let
G
be a pro-semisimple group whose neutral component is simply connected. Let
H
be a finite-type quotient of
G
. Then there is a
finite type quotient
H^{\prime}
of
G
whose neutral component is simply connected such that the map
G\to H
factors through
H^{\prime}
.

Proof.

Let
K
denote the neutral component of the kernel of
G\to H
. Note that
K
is a normal subgroup of
G
. Because
G^{\circ}
was assumed to be simply connected,
G^{\circ}/K
is the simply-connected cover of
H^{\circ}
. For every nonzero element
x\in G/K
, there is some intermediate finite-type quotient
G/K\to H_{x}\to H
such that the image of
x
is nonzero in
H_{x}
. Because the kernel of
G^{\circ}/K\to H^{\circ}
is finite, there is some finite-type quotient
H^{\prime}
of
G/K
such that
(H^{\prime})^{\circ}=G^{\circ}/K
is the simply-connected cover of
H
, and such that
G\to H
factors through
H^{\prime}
.
∎

5.8.6.
Step 2

We now prove Proposition
5.7.7
for any
H
.

By
[
Dr
, Proposition 3.1.4]
and Lemma
5.8.5
, we can find a quotient
H^{\prime}
of
G
such that
(H^{\prime})^{\circ}
is simply connected and the map
G\to H
factors through
H^{\prime}
.

By Step 1, we know that there is an open subgroup
U
of
\operatorname{Gal}({\mathbb{Q}})
such that the map
G\to H^{\prime}
is
U
-equivariant, where
H^{\prime}
has the split equivariance structure.

Since both
H^{\prime}
and
H
have the split equivariance structure, after replacing
U
by a finite index open subgroup the map
H^{\prime}\to H
is
U
-equivariant as well.

∎[Proposition
5.7.7
]
